\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]\doteq\big(T-\boldsymbol{\sigma}\big[\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}],A,f_{l}\big]\big)\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B),

while, if
\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]\in\,]0,T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)[\,
, we set

(4.4)

\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]\doteq\big(T-\boldsymbol{\tau}\big[\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}],B,f_{r}\big]\big)\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A).

where
\boldsymbol{\sigma}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]
,
\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
, denote the shock starting
times introduced in
§
3.4
-
3.5
.
Recalling (
3.15
), (
3.32
), (
3.39
), we can extend by continuity the definitions (
4.3
), (
4.4
),
setting

(4.5)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]

\displaystyle\doteq 0,\qquad\text{if}\qquad\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]=0\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]

\displaystyle\doteq 0,\qquad\text{if}\qquad R[\omega,f_{r}]=0\,.

Such quantities are used to express the pointwise constraints satisfied by
\omega
in intervals containing the origin
whenever
\omega
is attainable.
Next, to express the Oleǐnik-type inequalities satisfied by
the attainable profiles it is useful to introduce the functions,

(4.6)

\displaystyle g[\omega,f_{l},f_{r}](x)

\displaystyle\doteq\!\dfrac{f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(x))\left[f^{\prime}_{r}\circ\pi_{r,-}^{l}(\omega(x))\right]^{2}}{\left[f^{\prime\prime}_{r}\circ\pi_{r,-}^{l}(\omega(x))\right]\!\left[f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(x))\right]^{2}\!\big(T\!\cdot\!f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(x))-x\big)+x\big[f^{\prime}_{r}\circ\pi_{r,-}^{l}(\omega(x))\big]^{2}\!f^{\prime\prime}_{l}(\omega(x))},

\displaystyle h[\omega,f_{l},f_{r}](x)

\displaystyle\doteq\!\dfrac{f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(x))\big[f^{\prime}_{l}\circ\pi_{l,+}^{r}(\omega(x))\big]^{2}}{\big[f^{\prime\prime}_{l}\circ\pi_{l,+}^{r}(\omega(x))\big]\!\left[f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(x))\right]^{2}\big(T\!\cdot\!f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(x))-x\big)+x\big[f^{\prime}_{l}\circ\pi_{l,+}^{r}(\omega(x))\big]^{2}\!f^{\prime\prime}_{r}(\omega(x))},

defined for
x\in\,]\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}],0[
,

\omega(x)\leq A
, and
for

x\in\,]0,\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}][
,

\omega(x)\geq B
, respectively.

Remark 4.1
.

The definitions of the functions
g,h
are meaningful in their domains.
In fact, the maps
\pi_{r,-}^{l},

\pi_{l,+}^{r}
in (
4.1
)
(that appear in the definitions of
g,h
)
are well defined if

\omega(x)\leq A
, and
\omega(x)\geq B
, respectively.
Moreover, by definition (
4.2
), we have

\displaystyle Tf_{l}^{\prime}(\omega(x))-x<0,\qquad f_{l}^{\prime}(\omega(x))<0\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}],\,0[\,,

\displaystyle Tf_{r}^{\prime}(\omega(x))-x>0,\qquad f_{r}^{\prime}(\omega(x))>0\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]0,\,\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}][\,.

Hence, relying also on (
1.4
), we deduce that the denominator of
g
is strictly negative for
x\in\,]\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}],0[
, while
the denominator of
h
is strictly positive for
x\in\,]0,\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}][
.
The functions
g,h
will provide
a one-sided upper bound for the derivative of
\omega
only in the interval
\,]\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}],0[
,
assuming
\omega(x)\leq A
, and
on the interval
]0,\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}][
, assuming

\omega(x)\geq B
, respectively.

Since by Remark
2.3
we know that

\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)\subset BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\})
,
we can partition the attainable set as

(4.7)

\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)=\bigcup_{\mathsf{L}\leq 0,\,\mathsf{R}\geq 0}\big(\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)\cap\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}\big),

where

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{\omega\in(\mathbf{L^{\infty}}\cap BV_{\mathrm{loc}})(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\})\;:\;\ \mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]=\mathsf{L},\quad\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]=\mathsf{R}\Big\}\,.

The characterization of the attainable profiles
in
\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}
will be given in:

-

Theorem
4.3
, if
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
, and
(A,B)
is non critical;

-

Theorem
4.9
, if
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
, and
(A,B)
is critical;

-

Theorem
4.11
, if
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}=0
or
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
;

-

Theorem
4.14
, if
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}=0
.

Remark 4.2
.

Any element of
\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}

is an equivalence class of functions that
admit one-sided limit at any point
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
and that have at most countably many discontinuities.
Therefore, for any element of
\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}
, we can always choose a representative which is left or right continuous. For sake of uniqueness, throughout the paper we will
consider a representative of
\omega
that is right continuous.

Throughout the following

(4.9)

D^{-}\omega(x)=\liminf_{h\rightarrow 0}\frac{\omega(x+h)-\omega(x)}{h},\quad\quad D^{+}\omega(x)=\limsup_{h\rightarrow 0}\frac{\omega(x+h)-\omega(x)}{h},

will denote, respectively, the lower and
the upper Dini derivative of a function
\omega
at
x
.

Theorem 4.3
.

In the same setting of Theorem
1.1
,
let
(A,B)
be a non critical connection,
let
\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)
,
T>0
, be the set
in (
1.5
), and
let
\omega
be an element of the
set
\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}

in (
4.8
),
with
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
.
Then,

\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)
if and only if the limits
\omega(0\pm)
exist, and there
hold:

(i)

the following Oleǐnik-type inequalities are satisfied

(4.10)

\displaystyle D^{+}\omega(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime\prime}(\omega(x))}\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]\!-\!\infty,\;\mathsf{L}[\,,

\displaystyle D^{+}\omega(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime\prime}(\omega(x))}\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[\,.

Moreover,
letting
g,h
be the functions in (
4.6
),
and letting

\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\doteq\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}\doteq\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]
,
be the constants in (
4.3
),
(
4.4
),
if
\mathsf{R}\in\,]0,T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[
, and if
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}>{\mathsf{L}}
, then one has

(4.11)

D^{+}\omega(x)\leq g[\omega,f_{l},f_{r}](x)\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]\mathsf{L},\,\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,[\,,

while, if
\mathsf{L}\in\,]T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A),0[
, and if
\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}<{\mathsf{R}}
, then one has

(4.12)

D^{+}\omega(x)\leq h[\omega,f_{l},f_{r}](x)\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]\widetilde{\mathsf{R}},\;\mathsf{R}[\,.

(ii)

letting
\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
,
\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]
, be
constants defined as
in (
3.7
),
(
3.11
),
the following pointwise state constraints are satisfied

(4.13)

\begin{aligned} \mathsf{L}\in\,]T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A),0[\qquad&\Longrightarrow\qquad\omega(\mathsf{L}-)\geq\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]\geq\omega(\mathsf{L}+)\,,\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\cr\mathsf{R}\in\,]0,T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[\qquad&\Longrightarrow\qquad\omega(\mathsf{R}+)\leq\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\leq\omega(\mathsf{R}-)\,.\end{aligned}\qquad

(4.14)

\displaystyle\Big[\mathsf{L}\in\,]T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A),0[\,\;\ \mathrm{and}\;\ \mathsf{R}\leq\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}\,\Big]\quad\mathrm{or}\quad\ \mathsf{L}\leq T\!\cdot\!f_{l}^{\prime}(A)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\omega(x)=B\qquad\forall~x\in\,]0,\mathsf{R}[\,,\qquad

\displaystyle\Big[\mathsf{R}\in\,]0,T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[\,\;\ \mathrm{and}\;\ \widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\leq\mathsf{L}\,\Big]\quad\mathrm{or}\quad\mathsf{R}\geq T\!\cdot\!f_{r}^{\prime}(B)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\omega(x)=A\qquad\forall~x\in\,]\mathsf{L},0[\,,\qquad

(4.15)

\displaystyle\hskip 43.36243pt\mathsf{L}\in\,]T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A),0[\;\ \mathrm{and}\;\ \widetilde{\mathsf{R}}<\mathsf{R}\qquad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\left\{\begin{aligned} \omega(x)&=B\qquad\forall~x\in\,]0,\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}\,],\\
\omega(\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}+)&=B,\\
\omega(x)&\geq B\qquad\forall~x\in\,]\,\widetilde{\mathsf{R}},\,\mathsf{R}[\,,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.16)

\displaystyle\hskip 43.36243pt\mathsf{R}\in\,]0,T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[\,\;\ \mathrm{and}\;\ \mathsf{L}<\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\qquad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\left\{\begin{aligned} \omega(x)&=A\qquad\forall~x\in[\,\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},\,0[\,,\\
\omega(\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}-)&=A,\\
\omega(x)&\leq A\qquad\forall~x\in\,]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,[\,,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.17)

\quad\ \begin{aligned} \mathsf{L}\leq T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A)\qquad&\Longrightarrow\qquad\omega(\mathsf{L}-)\geq\omega(\mathsf{L}+),\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\mathsf{R}\geq T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)\qquad&\Longrightarrow\qquad\omega(\mathsf{R}-)\geq\omega(\mathsf{R}+).\end{aligned}

\mathsf{L}
\omega
x
t
\mathsf{R}
\mathbf{\tau}[\mathsf{R},\ B,\ f_{r}]
A
B
\tilde{\mathsf{L}}

Figure 6
.
Case 1.

\mathsf{L}
\omega
x
t

\mathbf{\tau}[\mathsf{R},\ B,\ f_{r}]

\overline{A}

.

\mathsf{L}

\mathsf{R}
\mathbf{\tau}[\mathsf{R},\ B,\ f_{r}]
A

\tilde{\mathsf{L}}

.

\omega

.
Case 4.

Remark 4.4
.

Notice that conditions (
4.14
), (
4.15
) imply
\omega(\mathsf{R}-)\geq B
.
On the other hand, if
\mathsf{R}<T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)
,
by virtue of (
4.13
),
and because of (
3.31
), we have

\omega(\mathsf{R}+)\leq B
.
Hence, because of (
4.17
), it follows that
the inequality
\omega(\mathsf{R}-)\geq\omega(\mathsf{R}+)
is always
satisfied.
With similar arguments we deduce that also
the inequality

\omega(\mathsf{L}-)\geq\omega(\mathsf{L}+)
is always verified.

Remark 4.5
.

If

\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]\in\,]0,T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)[
,
applying (
3.38
)
with
f_{r}
in place of
f
and
\mathsf{R}=\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]
,
and recalling (
4.3
),
we derive

(4.18)

\frac{\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]}{f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}<T-\boldsymbol{\tau}\big[\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}],B,f_{r}\big]=\frac{\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]}{f^{\prime}_{l}(A)}\,.

Similarly, if
\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]\in\,]T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A),0[
, applying (
3.43
)
with
f_{l}
in place of
f
and
\mathsf{L}=\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]
,
and recalling that
f^{\prime}_{l}(A)<0

we find

(4.19)

\frac{\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]}{f^{\prime}_{l}(A)}<T-\boldsymbol{\sigma}\big[\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}],A,f_{l}\big]\,.

Hence, if
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]\geq\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]
, combining (
4.18
),
(
4.19
), we deduce

(4.20)

\frac{\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]}{f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}<T-\boldsymbol{\sigma}\big[\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}],A,f_{l}\big]\,,

which, in turn, by (
4.3
) yields

(4.21)

\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]<\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]\,.

With entirely similar arguments one can show that, if
\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]\leq\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]
,
then one has

(4.22)

\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]>\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]\,.

Therefore, when
\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]\in\,]T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A),0[
,  and
\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]\in\,]0,T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)[
,
we have

(4.23)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]\geq\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]>\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]\leq\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]<\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]\,.

These implications, in particular, show that it can never occur the case where

(4.24)

\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]\geq\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]\qquad\text{and}\qquad\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]\leq\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]\,.

Remark 4.6
.

Notice that by condition (
4.14
) in Theorem
4.3
,
and because of (
4.3
),
it follows that
if
\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]\in\,]T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A),0[\,
, and

\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]\leq\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]
,
then one has

\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]<T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)
.
Therefore, we have

(4.25)

\Big[\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]\in\,]T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A),0[\,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]\geq T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)\Big]\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]>\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]\,.

Similarly, one can show that,
by (
4.3
), (
4.14
), we have

(4.26)

\Big[\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]\in\,]0,T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)[\,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]\leq T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A)\Big]\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]<\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]\,.

Then, relying on (
4.23
),
(
4.25
), (
4.26
), we deduce
that, for non critical connections,
we can distinguish
six cases
of pointwise constraints prescribed by condition (ii)
of Theorem
4.3
,
which depend on the reciprocal positions of the points

\mathsf{L}=\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]
,
\mathsf{R}=\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]
,
and

\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}=\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]
,

\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}=\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]
:

Case 1:

If
\mathsf{L}\leq T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A)<0
,
0<\mathsf{R}<T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)
(Figure
6
), then
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}>\mathsf{L}
, and
it holds true

(4.27)

\omega(\mathsf{L}-)\geq\omega(\mathsf{L}+)\,,\qquad\omega(x)\leq A\quad\ \forall~x\in\,]{\mathsf{L}},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,[\,,\qquad\ \ \omega(\,\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}-)=A,\qquad\ \ \omega(x)=A\quad\ \forall~x\in\,]\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},0[\,,

(4.28)

\omega(x)=B\ \quad\ \forall~x\in\,]0,{\mathsf{R}}[\,,\qquad\ \ \omega(\mathsf{R}+)\leq\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\leq B\,;

Case 2:
If

T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A)<\mathsf{L}<0
,
0<\mathsf{R}<T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)
, and
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}>\mathsf{L}
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}>\mathsf{R}
(Figure
7
), then
it holds true (
4.28
) and

(4.29)

\omega(\mathsf{L}-)\geq\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]\geq A\,,\quad\ \ \omega(x)\leq A\quad\forall~x\in\,]{\mathsf{L}},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,[\,,\quad\ \ \omega(\,\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}-)=A,\quad\ \ \omega(x)=A\quad\forall~x\in\,]\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},0[\,;

the symmetric ones:

Case 1b:

If
T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A)<\mathsf{L}<0
,
0<T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)\leq\mathsf{R}
, then
\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}<\mathsf{R}
and it holds true that

(4.30)

\omega(x)=A\qquad\forall~x\in\,]{\mathsf{L}},0[\,,\qquad\ \omega(\mathsf{L}-)\geq\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]\geq A\,,

(4.31)

\omega(x)=B\quad\ \forall~x\in\,]0,\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}\,[\,,\qquad\ \ \omega(\,\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}+)=B,\qquad\ \ \omega(x)\geq B\quad\ \forall~x\in\,]\widetilde{\mathsf{R}},\mathsf{R}[\,;

Case 2b:

If
T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A)<\mathsf{L}<0
,
0<\mathsf{R}<T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)
, and
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}<\mathsf{L}
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}<\mathsf{R}
, then it holds true (
4.30
) and

(4.32)

\omega(x)=B\quad\forall~x\in\,]0,\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}\,[\,,\quad\ \ \omega(\,\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}+)=B,\quad\ \ \omega(x)\geq B\quad\forall~x\in\,]\widetilde{\mathsf{R}},\mathsf{R}[\,\quad\ \ \omega(\mathsf{R}+)\leq\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\leq B\,;

and the remaining ones:

Case 3:

If

T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A)<\mathsf{L}<0
,
0<\mathsf{R}<T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)
, and
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\leq\mathsf{L}
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}\geq\mathsf{R}
(Figure
8
),
then it holds true (
4.28
), (
4.30
);

Case 4:

If
\mathsf{L}\leq T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A)<0
and
\mathsf{R}\geq T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)>0

(Figure
9
),
then it holds true

(4.33)

\displaystyle\omega(x)=A\qquad\forall~x\in\,]{\mathsf{L}},0[\,,\qquad\quad\omega(\mathsf{L}-)\geq\omega(\mathsf{L}+)\,,

\displaystyle\omega(x)=B\ \quad\ \forall~x\in\,]0,{\mathsf{R}}[\,,\qquad\quad\omega(\mathsf{R}-)\geq\omega(\mathsf{R}+)\,.

The six cases are depicted in Figure
10
. One can regard the intervals
]T\!\cdot\!f_{l}^{\prime}(A),0[

and
]0,T\!\cdot\!f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[
as “
active zones
”
for the presence of shocks in an
AB
-entropy solution
that attains
\omega

at time
T
: as soon as
\mathsf{L}

belongs to

]T\!\cdot\!f_{l}^{\prime}(A),0[
or
\mathsf{R}

belongs to
]0,T\!\cdot\!f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[
, it is needed
a shock located in
\{x<0\}

or in
\{x>0\}
, respectively,

in order to produce the discontinuity occurring in
\omega
at
\mathsf{L}
or
\mathsf{R}
.

Remark 4.7
.

When the connection is not critical and

\mathsf{L}\doteq\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]<0
,

\omega(x)=A\qquad\forall~x\in\,]{\mathsf{L}},0[\,,\qquad\qquad\omega(x)=B\qquad\forall~x\in\,]0,\mathsf{R}[\,.

Therefore, such a characterization
in particular excludes all attainable profiles
\omega

) or (

), (

),
of Cases 1B and 2B,
with

\omega(x){~>~}B\qquad\text{{ for some}}\ \ x\in\,]\widetilde{\mathsf{R}},{\mathsf{R}}[\,.

Remark 4.8
.

Notice that, if
A=\theta_{l}
, or
\mathsf{R}=0
,
by definition (
4.4
),
and because of (
4.5
), it follows that

\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}=0
.
Similarly, if
B=\theta_{r}
, or
\mathsf{L}=0
,
we have
\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}=0
.
Thus, in the case of critical connections, or whenever
\mathsf{L}=0

or
\mathsf{R}=0
(for critical and non critical connections),
the characterization
of the profiles

\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{AB}(T)\cap\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}
will not involve the constants
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}
.

.

Remark 4.10
.

For critical connections,
whenever
\mathsf{L}<0<\mathsf{R}

we can distinguish
two cases
of pointwise constraints prescribed by Theorem
4.9

on an attainable profile
\omega
,
which depend on the side in which
the connection is critical.

Case 1:

If
A=\theta_{l}
, and
\mathsf{L}<0<\mathsf{R}<T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)
(Figure
11
), then it holds true

(f^{\prime}_{l})^{-1}\bigg(\frac{x}{T-\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]}\bigg)\leq\omega(x)<\theta_{l},\qquad\forall~x\in\,]\mathsf{L},0[\,,\qquad\ \ \omega(0-)=\theta_{l}\,,

\omega(x)=B\ \quad\ \forall~x\in\,]0,{\mathsf{R}}[\,,\qquad\ \ \omega(\mathsf{R}+)\leq\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\leq B\,;

Case 2:

If
B=\theta_{r}
, and
T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A)<\mathsf{L}<0<\mathsf{R}
, then it holds true

\omega(x)=A\ \quad\ \forall~x\in\,]{\mathsf{L}},0[\,,\qquad\ \ A\geq\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]\geq\omega(\mathsf{L}+)\,;

\theta_{r}<\omega(x)\leq(f^{\prime}_{r})^{-1}\bigg(\frac{x}{T-\boldsymbol{\sigma}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]}\bigg)\qquad\forall~x\in\,]0,\mathsf{R}[\,,\qquad\ \ \omega(0+)=\theta_{r}\,.

In both cases an
AB
-entropy solution
that attains
\omega
at time
T
must contain a shock located in
\{x>0\}

(in
Case 1
),
or in
\{x<0\}
(in
Case 2
), in order to produce the discontinuity occurring in
\omega
at
\mathsf{R}
or

\mathsf{L}
.

Theorem 4.11
.

In the same setting of Theorem
4.3
,

\omega

),

),

,

),

).

,

,

\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)
if and only if the limits
\omega(0\pm)
exist, and it

(4.46)

\displaystyle D^{+}\omega(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime\prime}(\omega(x))}\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]\!-\!\infty,\;\mathsf{L}[\,,

\displaystyle D^{+}\omega(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime\prime}(\omega(x))}\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]0,+\infty[\,,

the following pointwise state constraints are satisfied:

\begin{cases}\omega(x)\leq A\ \ &\text{if}\quad A<\theta_{l},\\
\omega(x)<A\ \ &\text{if}\quad A=\theta_{l},\end{cases}\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]\mathsf{L},0[\,,

(4.49)

\omega(\mathsf{0}+)\leq\pi^{l}_{r,-}(\omega(0-))\,,

and

(4.51)

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}~{\leq}~T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A)\qquad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\omega(\mathsf{L}-)\geq\omega(\mathsf{L}+).

,

, then

if and only if it holds true:

′

the following Oleǐnik-type inequalities are satisfied

(4.52)

\displaystyle D^{+}\omega(x)

\displaystyle D^{+}\omega(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime\prime}(\omega(x))}\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[\,,

(4.53)

D^{+}\omega(x)\leq h[\omega,f_{l},f_{r}](x)\qquad\forall\;x\in\ ]0,\mathsf{R}[\,.\quad\

(ii)
′

the following pointwise state constraints are satisfied:

(4.54)

\begin{cases}\omega(x)\geq B\ \ &\text{if}\quad B>\theta_{r},\\
\omega(x)>B\ \ &\text{if}\quad B=\theta_{r},\end{cases}\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]0,\mathsf{R}[\,,

(4.55)

\omega(\mathsf{0}-)\geq\pi^{r}_{l,+}(\omega(0+))\,,

and

(4.56)

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}\in\,]0,T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[\,\qquad

(4.57)

Remark 4.12
.

Notice that
the implications (
4.50
)-(
4.51
),
(
4.56
)-(
4.57
) can be extended to
\mathsf{L}=T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A)
and to
\mathsf{R}=T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)
, respectively.
In fact,
by definition (
4.2
) of

\mathsf{L}=\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]
, one has
f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(\mathsf{L}-))\geq\mathsf{L}/T
.
Hence, if
\mathsf{L}=T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A)
it follows that

f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(\mathsf{L}-))\geq f^{\prime}_{l}(A)
which yields

\omega(\mathsf{L}-)\geq A

by the monotonicity of
f^{\prime}_{l}
.
Thus, recalling that by (
3.30
)
we have
\boldsymbol{v}[T\cdot f^{\prime}(A),A,f]=A
, we derive

(4.58)

\omega(T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A)-)\geq\boldsymbol{v}[T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A),A,f_{l}]\,.

On the other hand,
since (
4.48
) implies
\omega(T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A+)\leq A
,
we deduce from (
4.58
) that

(4.59)

\omega(T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A)-)\geq\omega(T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(A)+)\,.

With entirely similar arguments one can show that we have

(4.60)

\displaystyle\omega(T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(B)+)

\displaystyle\leq\boldsymbol{u}[T\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(B),B,f_{r}]\,,

(4.61)

\displaystyle\omega(T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)+)

\displaystyle\leq\omega(T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)-)\,.

Hence, relying on (
4.48
), (
4.50
), (
4.51
),
(
4.54
),
(
4.56
),
(
4.57
),
and on (
4.59
), (
4.61
),
with the same arguments of Remark
4.4
we deduce that the
inequalities
\omega(\mathsf{L}-)\geq\omega(\mathsf{L}+)
,

\omega(\mathsf{R}-)\geq\omega(\mathsf{R}+)

are always satisfied.

Remark 4.13
.

Relying on Remark
4.8
, we can view
the conditions that characterize the pointwise constraints
of attainable profiles in Theorem
4.11
as limiting cases of
the conditions of Theorems
4.3
,
4.9
,
classified in Remarks
4.6
,
4.10
. Namely:

-

For non critical connections, the case
\mathsf{L}\in\,]T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A),0[\,
,
\mathsf{R}=0
(Figure
12
), is the limiting situation
as
\mathsf{R}\to 0
of
Case 2
in Remark
4.6
.
For critical connections with
A<\theta_{l}
,
B=\theta_{r}
, if the constraint (
4.48
)
is satisfied with the equality,
the case
\mathsf{L}\in\,]T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A),0[\,
,
\mathsf{R}=0
, is the limiting situation
as
\mathsf{R}\to 0

of
Case 2
in Remark
4.10
.

-

For non critical connections, the case
\mathsf{L}\leq T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A)
,
\mathsf{R}=0
(Figure
13
), is the limiting situation as
\mathsf{R}\to 0
of
Case 1

in Remark
4.6
.
For critical connections with
A=\theta_{l}
,
B>\theta_{r}
,
the case
\mathsf{L}\leq T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A)
,
\mathsf{R}=0
, is the limiting situation as
\mathsf{R}\to 0
of

Case 1
in Remark
4.10

Symmetrically, we have:

-

For non critical connections, the case
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}\in\,]0,T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[\,
, is the limiting situation
as
\mathsf{L}\to 0
of
Case 2B
in Remark
4.6
.
For critical connections with
A=\theta_{l}
,
B>\theta_{r}
,
if the constraint (
4.54
)
is satisfied with the equality,
the case
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}\in\,]0,T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[\,
is the limiting situation
as
\mathsf{L}\to 0
of
Case 1
in Remark
4.10
.

-

For non critical connections, the case
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}\geq T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)
, is the limiting situation
as
\mathsf{L}\to 0
of
Case 1B
in Remark
4.6
.
For critical connections with
A<\theta_{l}
,
B=\theta_{r}
,
the case
\mathsf{R}\geq T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)
is the limiting situation
as
\mathsf{L}\to 0
of

in Remark

.

or of

.

-entropy solution
that attains at time

a profile

.

(in

.

(i)

\displaystyle D^{+}\omega(x)

\displaystyle D^{+}\omega(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime\prime}(\omega(x))}\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]0,+\infty[\,.

(ii)

the following pointwise state constraints are satisfied:

Remark 4.15
.

Recalling that by (
3.30
) we have
\boldsymbol{v}[0,A,f_{l}]=\overline{A}
,
\boldsymbol{u}[0,B,f_{r}]=\overline{B}
, we can rephrase the constraint (
4.63
) as

(4.64)

\omega(\mathsf{0}-)\geq~\boldsymbol{v}[0,A,f_{l}],\qquad\omega(\mathsf{0}+)\leq~\boldsymbol{u}[0,B,f_{r}]\,.

Any profile
\omega

satisfying the conditions of Theorem
4.14
is attainable by
AB
-entropy solutions that
don’t contain shocks in
\{x<0\}

or in
\{x>0\}.

Since by Lemma
3.1
we have

\lim_{\mathsf{R}\to 0^{+}}\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},f_{r},B]=\overline{B},\qquad\lim_{\mathsf{R}\to 0^{-}}\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},f_{l},A]=\overline{A},

and because of Remark 4.8,
we can recover the conditions that characterize the pointwise constraints
of attainable profiles in Theorem
4.14

as limiting cases of the conditions of Theorems
4.3
,
4.9
, classified in Remarks
4.6
,
4.10
. Namely:

-

For a non critical connection,
the condition (
4.63
) is the limit situation as
\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\to 0
of
the CASE 2 of Remark
4.6
.

-

For a critical connection with
A=\theta_{l}
,
B>\theta_{r}
,
the second condition of (
4.63
) is
is the limiting situation
as
\mathsf{R}\to 0

of
Case 1
in Remark
4.10
.
The first condition of (
4.63
)
is trivially satisfied, because
\overline{A}=\theta_{l}
, and since
\mathsf{L}=0

by definition (
4.2
)
implies

\omega(0-)\geq\theta_{l}
.
The case of a critical connection with
A<\theta_{l}
,
B=\theta_{r}
is symmetric,
and can be recovered as
limiting situation
as
\mathsf{L}\to 0

of
Case 2
in Remark
4.10
.

Remark 4.16
.

By Remarks
4.13
,
4.15
, the conditions that characterize the pointwise constraints of attainable profiles provided by Theorems
4.3
,
4.9
are essentially “dense” in the set of all conditions
characterizing the pointwise constraints of any profile
\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)

(in the sense that the further conditions
provided by Theorems
4.11
,
4.14

can be recovered via a limiting procedure as
the parameters
\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\to 0
).

Combining Theorems
4.3
,
4.9
,
4.11
,
4.14
, with Theorem
1.1
, we obtain:

Theorem 4.17
.

In the same setting of Theorem
1.1
, let

(A,B)
be
a connection. Then,
for every
T>0
,
and for any
\omega\in{\bf L^{\infty}}(\mathbb{R})
,
the following conditions are equivalent.

(1)

\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{AB}(T)
.

(2)

\mathcal{{\mathcal{S}}_{\it T}^{\,[{\it AB}\,]+}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{T}^{\,[AB]-}\omega=\omega\,
.

(3)

\omega
is an element of
the set
\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}
in (
4.8
), with
\mathsf{L}\leq 0
,
\mathsf{R}\geq 0
, that
satisfies the conditions of Theorem
4.3
,

4.9
,
4.11
, or
4.14
.

Moreover, if
(A,B)
is a non critical connection, i.e. if
A\neq\theta_{l},B\neq\theta_{r}
, then the conditions (2) and (3) are equivalent to

(1)’

\omega\in\mathcal{A}_{bv}^{[AB]}(T)
, where

(4.65)

\mathcal{A}_{bv}^{[AB]}(T)\doteq\big\{\mathcal{{\mathcal{S}}_{\it T}^{\,[{\it AB}\,]+}}u_{0}\;:\;u_{0}\in BV_{loc}(\mathbb{R})\big\}\,,

and it holds true

(4.66)

\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)=\mathcal{A}_{bv}^{[AB]}(T)\,.

Remark 4.18

(Comparison with previous results)
.

Theorems
4.3
,
4.9
,
4.11
,
4.14
yield the first
complete
characterization of the attainable set at time
T>0

in terms of Oleǐnik-type inequalities and unilateral constraints
,
for critical and non critical connections.
Partial results in this direction have been
recently obtained for
strict subsets
of
\mathcal{A}^{AB}(T)
.
In particular, we refer to:

•

the work
[
6
]
, where it is characterized
only the subset
{\mathcal{A}}_{\rm L}^{AB}(T)\subset\mathcal{A}^{AB}(T)
given by

\quad\ \ {\mathcal{A}}_{\rm L}^{[AB]}(T)=\{\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)\;|\;\exists\;\text{$AB$-entropy solution}\;u\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\})\;:\;u(T,x)=\omega\}.

In particular, all the profiles
\omega
for which
\mathsf{L}\in\,]T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A),0[\,
or
\mathsf{R}\in\,]0,T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[\,
are missing in the characterization provided in
[
6
]
.
In fact, as observed in Remarks
4.6
,

4.10
,

4.13
,
an
AB
-entropy solutions leading to such profiles at time
T

must contain
a shock located in
\{x<0\}

or in
\{x>0\}
, respectively,
in order to produce the discontinuity occurring in
\omega
at
\mathsf{L}
or
\mathsf{R}
.

•

the work
[
2
]
,
in which, whenever either
\mathsf{L}=0
, or
\mathsf{R}=0
,
the set

\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)
is fully characterized
in terms
of triples (a monotone function and a pair of points)
related to the
Lax-Oleinik representation formula of solutions (obtained in
[
5
]
via
the Hamiton-Jacobi dual formulation).
Instead, in the case of critical connections, all attainable profiles
with
\mathsf{L}<0
and
\mathsf{R}>0

described by Theorem
4.9

are missing in
[
2
]
.
On the other hand,
when
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0

and
(A,B)
,
is a non critical connection,
only the profiles of

Cases 3, 4
, discussed in Remark
4.5
, are characterized in
[
2
]
, while the ones of
Cases 1, 2, 1b, 2b
are missing. In fact, the profiles constructed in
[
2
]
with
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
for non critical connections, satisfy always the condition

\omega(x)=A
for all
x\in(\mathsf{L},0)
, and
\omega(x)=B
for all
x\in(0,\mathsf{R})
,
which is in general not fulfilled by profiles of
Cases 1, 2, 1b, 2b

(cfr. Remark
4.7
).

We point out that, as a byproduct of
the characterization
of
\mathcal{A}^{AB}(T)
via Oleǐnik-type estimates, one can establish
uniform BV bounds on solutions to (
1.1
), (
1.3
)
in the case of non critical connections,
and on the flux of solutions to (
1.1
), (
1.3
)
for general connections (see. Proposition
6.1
in Appendix
A
).
In turn such bounds yield the

{\mathbf{L}^{1}_{loc}}
-Lipschitz continuity
in time of
AB
-entropy solutions
(see the proof of Theorem
2.8
-
(v)
)
in Appendix
A
).

5.
Proof of Theorem
4.17

5.1.
Proof roadmap

Observe that if
(A,B)
is a non critical connection, then recalling Definition
2.16
,
and relying on Proposition
6.1

in Appendix
A
,
we deduce that
\mathcal{S}_{T}^{\,[AB]-}\omega\in BV_{loc}(\mathbb{R})

for all
\omega\in{\bf L^{\infty}}(\mathbb{R})
.
Hence setting
u_{0}\doteq\mathcal{S}_{T}^{\,[AB]-}\omega
,
we deduce immediately the implication

(2)\Rightarrow(1)^{\prime}
.
On the other hand, since

\mathcal{A}_{bv}^{[AB]}(T)\subset\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)
, from the implication

(1)\Rightarrow(3)
, one deduces that

(1)^{\prime}\Rightarrow(3)
holds as well.

Therefore,
in order to establish Theorem
4.17

it will be sufficient to prove the equivalence of the conditions (1), (2), (3).
We provide here a road map of the proof of
(1)\Rightarrow(2)\Rightarrow(3)\Rightarrow(1)
.
There are three main parts, which are somewhat independent one from the other.

Part 1.

The case of a non critical connection
{\bf(1)}\Rightarrow{\bf(3)}
.

In Sections
5.2
-
5.3
we prove the implication
(1)\Rightarrow(3)
of Theorem
4.17

when
(A,B)
is a
non critical
connection.
The proof has a bootstrap-like structure,
and it is divided in two steps.
We first prove that
{\bf(1)}\Rightarrow{\bf(3)}
under the regularity assumption (
H
) formulated below, and next we show that this regularity property always holds true.

\bullet

5.2.
Part 1.a -

(1)\Rightarrow(3)
for non critical connections assuming (
)
.

This is the
first fundamental block
of our proof.
We prove in §
5.2
the implication
(1)\Rightarrow(3)
for profiles
\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)
that satisfy the BV condition:

(H)

\quad\exists~u_{0}\in{\bf L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\ :\ \omega={\mathcal{S}}_{\it T}^{\,[{\it AB}\,]+}u_{0}\,,\quad\ \text{and}\quad\ \mathcal{S}_{t}^{\,[AB]+}u_{0}\in BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R})\quad\forall~t>0\,.

The derivation of
the conditions of Theorem
4.3
,
4.11
, and
4.14

is obtained exploiting
as in
[
6
]
the non crossing property of genuine characteristics
in the domains
\{x>0,\,t>0\}
,

\{x<0,\,t>0\}
,
together with the non existence of
rarefactions emanating from the interface (cfr. Appendix
B
and
[
2
]
).
Two key novel points of the analysis here are:

- a blowup argument, possible thanks to assumption (
H
), to
derive the Oleǐnik-type inequalities
satisfied by
\omega
in regions comprising points
with characteristics reflected by the interface
x=0
, and points with characteristics refracted by

x=0
.

- a comparison argument
(based on the duality of forward and backward shocks of §
3.3
, and on the property of the states
\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
,
\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]
, defined in §
3.1
,
3.2
)
to establish the unilateral inequalities satisfied by
\omega

at points of discontinuity generated by shocks that
isolate
the interface

\{x=0\}

from the semiaxes
\{x<0\}
,
\{x>0\}
(cfr. Remark
3.3
).

\bullet

5.3.
Part 1.b -
(1)\Rightarrow(3)
for non critical connections without assuming (
)
.

We prove in §
5.3

the implication
(1)\Rightarrow(3)

for every
\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{AB}(T)

by showing that
every
\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{AB}(T)
actually
satisfies condition (H), and then the conclusion follows
by Part 1.a.
This is achieved: considering a sequence of functions
u_{n,0}\in BV(\mathbb{R})
that
\bf L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
-converge to
u_{0}\in{\bf L^{\infty}}(\mathbb{R})
;
observing that
\mathcal{S}_{t}^{\,[AB]+}u_{n,0}\in BV(\mathbb{R})
(see
[
1
,
29
]
);
deriving uniform BV bounds on
{\mathcal{S}}_{\it T}^{\,[{\it AB}\,]+}u_{n,0}
based on the Oleǐnik-type inequalities enjoyed by
{\mathcal{S}}_{\it T}^{\,[{\it AB}\,]+}u_{n,0}
because of Part 1.a;
relying on the
\bf L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
-stability
of the semigroup map
u_{0}\mapsto{\mathcal{S}}_{\it T}^{\,[{\it AB}\,]+}u_{0}
(see Theorem
2.8
-(iii)) and on the lower semicontinuity
of the total variation with respect to
{\bf L^{1}}
-convergence.

Part 2.

The case of a non critical connection
{\bf(3)}\Rightarrow{\bf(2)}\Rightarrow{\bf(1)}
.

The implication

(2)\Rightarrow(1)
of Theorem
4.17

immediately follows observing that,
by virtue of (2), one has

\omega={\mathcal{S}}_{\it T}^{\,[{\it AB}\,]+}u_{0}\in\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)
, with
u_{0}\doteq\mathcal{S}_{T}^{\,[AB]-}\omega
.
Hence, in Sections
5.4
-
5.5
we prove
only the implication
(3)\Rightarrow(2)
of Theorem
4.17
, in the case of a non critical connection
(A,B)
.
This is the
second fundamental block
of our proof,
which consists in first showing that
(3)\Rightarrow(1)
, and next in proving that
(3)\Rightarrow(2)
.

\bullet

5.4.
Part 2.a -
(3)\Rightarrow(1)
for non critical connections.
.

Given
\omega\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}

satisfying the condition of Theorem
4.3
,
we construct explicitly
in §
5.4

an
AB
-entropy admissible solution
u(x,t)\doteq\mathcal{S}_{t}^{[AB]+}u_{0}(x)
,
u_{0}\in{\bf L^{\infty}}(\mathbb{R})
, such that
u(\cdot,T)=\omega
.
The case where
\omega\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}

satisfies the condition of Theorem
4.11
, or
4.14
is entirely similar or simpler. The construction of
u_{0}
and
u
follows a by now standard procedure (see
[
6
]
,
[
8
]
)
in regions of
\{x<0\}
or of
\{x>0\}

that are not influenced by waves reflected or refracted by the interface
x=0
.
Namely, in these regions, one
construct the solution
u
along two type of lines that correspond to its characteristics:
genuine characteristics
\vartheta_{y}

ending at points
(y,T)
, where

u=\omega(y)
, in the case

\omega
is continuous at
y
;
compression fronts
\eta_{y,z}

connecting points
(z,0)
and
(y,T)
,
where
u=(f^{\prime}_{l})^{-1}(\frac{y-x}{T})
,
if
y<0
, and
u=(f^{\prime}_{r})^{-1}(\frac{y-x}{T})
,
if
y>0
,
in the case

\omega
is discontinuous at
y
.
A key novel point of the analysis here is the construction of
u
in two polygonal regions around the interface
x=0
,
which relies on the properties of
the
shock-rarefaction/rarefaction-shock wave patterns
established in §
3.4
-
3.5
,
which in turn are based on the
duality properties of forward/backward
shocks derived in
3.3
.
Thanks to this construction, one can in
particular explicitly produce
AB
-entropy solutions that attain at time
T

the profiles of
Cases 1, 2, 1b, 2b
discussed in Remark
4.5
, that are not present in
[
2
]

(cfr. Remark
4.18
).

\bullet

5.5.
Part 2.b -
(3)\Rightarrow(2)
for non critical connections.
.

Given
\omega\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}

satisfying the conditions of Theorem
4.3
, we show in §
5.5
that
\omega

is a fixed point of the
backward-forward operator
{\mathcal{S}}_{\it T}^{\,[{\it AB}\,]+}\circ\mathcal{S}_{T}^{\,[AB]-}
.
The case where
\omega\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}

satisfies the condition of Theorem
4.11
, or
4.14
is entirely similar.
Building on the analysis
pursued in the previous part,
in order to prove that

\omega=\mathcal{{\mathcal{S}}_{\it T}^{\,[{\it AB}\,]+}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{T}^{\,[AB]-}\omega

it is sufficient to show that, if
u_{0}
is the initial datum of the
AB
-entropy solution
u(x,t)
constructed in Part 2.a,
then one has
u_{0}=\mathcal{S}^{[AB]-}_{T}\omega
.
This is again achieved
exploiting the duality properties of forward/backward shocks derived in
3.3
,
and the structural properties of
the
shock-rarefaction/rarefaction-shock wave patterns
established in §
3.4
-
3.5
.

Part 3.

The case of a critical connection

{\bf(1)\Leftrightarrow(2)\Leftrightarrow(3)}
.

In Sections
5.6
,
5.7
,
5.8
we recover the equivalence of the conditions (1), (2), (3) of Theorem
4.17
in the case of critical
connections, invoking the validity of this equivalence for non critical connections established in Parts 1-2.
The proof is divided in three steps.

\bullet

5.6.
Part 3.a -
(1)\Leftrightarrow(2)
for critical connections
.

In §
5.6

we prove the implication
(1)\Rightarrow(2)
,
relying on the
\bf L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
-stability
of the maps
(A,B,u_{0})\mapsto{\mathcal{S}}_{\it T}^{\,[{\it AB}\,]+}u_{0}
,
(A,B,u_{0})\mapsto\mathcal{S}_{T}^{\,[AB]-}u_{0}
(see Theorem
2.8
-(iv)
and Definition
2.16
).
The reverse implication is immediate
as observed in Part 2.

\bullet

5.7.
Part 3.b -
(1)\Rightarrow(3)
for critical connections
.

In §
5.7

we prove the implication
(1)\Rightarrow(3)
, relying on the
{\bf L^{1}}
-weak stability
of the maps

(A,B)\mapsto f_{l}(u_{l})
,

(A,B)\mapsto f_{r}(u_{r})
,
where
u_{l},u_{r}
denote, respectively the left and right states of
u(x,t)\doteq\mathcal{S}_{t}^{\,[AB]+}u_{0}(x)
at
x=0
(see Corollary
2.11
), and on the lower/upper
{\bf L^{1}}
-semicontinuity property of solutions to conservation laws with uniformly convex flux (see Lemma
C.1
in Appendix
C
).

\bullet

5.8.
Part 3.c -
(3)\Rightarrow(1)
for critical connections
.

In §
5.8

we prove the implication

(3)\Rightarrow(1)
exploiting again the
{\bf L^{1}}_{\mathsf{l}oc}
-stability of the semigroup map of Theorem
2.8
-(iv), and using a perturbation argument.
Namely, given
\omega\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}

satisfying the conditions of Theorem
4.9
,
4.11
, or
4.14
, we construct a sequence
\{\omega_{n}\}_{n}

of perturbations
of
\omega

with the property that
\omega_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\bf L^{1}}}}{{\to}}\omega
,
and

\omega_{n}\in\mathcal{A}^{[A_{n}B_{n}]}(T)
, for
a sequence of non critical connections
\{(A_{n},B_{n})\}_{n}
.
This is another key point of our analysis, since it provides a general explicit procedure to approximate an attainable profile for a critical connection by attainable profiles for non critical connections.

Remark 5.1
.

In the case of critical connections,
one may provide a direct proof of the
implications
(2)\Rightarrow(1)
,

(3)\Rightarrow(1)
,
(3)\Rightarrow(2)
of Theorem
4.17
with similar arguments as the ones used in the case of non critical connections. Only the implication

(1)\Rightarrow(3)
in the case of
critical connections cannot be
directly established
with the same line of proof followed in
§
5.2
for non critical connection. The reason is twofold.
On one hand
we
cannot rely on the
property of non existence of
rarefactions emanating from the interface, since we establish in
Appendix
B

this property only in the case of non critical connections.
On the other hand
we cannot exploit
the uniform
BV_{\mathrm{loc}}
bounds to
perform the blowup argument of §
5.2.6
, since they are
enjoyed by
AB
-entropy solutions only when the connection is non critical
(see §
6
).
An alternative, direct proof of

(1)\Rightarrow(3)

can be obtained
relying on the property of preclusion of rarefactions emanating from the interface derived in
[
2
]

for general connections.
Using this property, it seems
reasonable that one may then establish
the Oleǐnik-type estimates
that characterize the attainable profiles
for critical connections performing a longer, technical analysis of
the structure of characteristics that avoids the blow up argument of §
5.2.6
.

5.2.
Part 1.a -

(1)\Rightarrow(3)
for non critical connections assuming (
H
)

In this Subsection,
given an element
\omega

of the set
\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)

for a non critical connection

(A,B)
, assuming
that

\omega
satisfies (
H
),
we will show that
\omega
fulfills condition (3) of Theorem
4.17
.
Recalling (
4.7
),
this is equivalent to show that,
letting

(5.1)

\mathsf{L}\doteq\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]\,,\qquad\quad\mathsf{R}\doteq\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}],

be quantities defined as in (
4.2
),
it holds true that:

2a-i)

If
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
, and if
\omega
satisfies (
H
), then
\omega
satisfies the conditions of Theorem
4.3
;

2a-ii)

If
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
or viceversa, and if
\omega
satisfies (
H
), then
\omega
satisfies the conditionsof Theorem
4.11
;

2a-iii)

If
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}=0
, then
\omega
satisfies the conditions of Theorem
4.14
.

We will prove 2a-i) in §
5.2.1
-
5.2.6
,
while 2a-ii) is proven in §
5.2.7
,
and 2a-iii) is discussed in §
5.2.8
.
The further assumption that
\omega
satisfies (
H
) is needed only to
ensure the existence of the one-sided limits

\omega(0\pm)
, and to show that
\omega
satisfies (
4.11
)-(
4.12
) in case 2a-i), and
(
4.47
), (
4.53
) in case 2a-ii).

Throughout the subsection we will assume that

(5.2)

\omega=\mathcal{S}_{T}^{[AB]+}u_{0},\qquad u_{0}\in\mathbf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\,,

and we set
u(x,t)\doteq S_{t}^{[AB]+}u_{0}(x)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
t\geq 0
.
Under assumption (
H
)
there exist the limits
u(0\pm,t)
, for all
t>0
.
We let
u_{l}(t),u_{r}(t)
denote the left and right traces
at
x=0
of
u(x,t)
,
t>0
.

5.2.1.
(
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
, proof of (
4.17
))

The inequalities
\omega(\mathsf{L}-)\geq\omega(\mathsf{L}+)
,

\omega(\mathsf{R}-)\geq\omega(\mathsf{R}+)

are the Lax conditions
which are satisfied since
u
is an entropy admissible solution of
the conservation law

u_{t}+f_{l}(u)_{x}=0
, on
x<0
,
and of

u_{t}+f_{r}(u)_{x}=0
, on
x>0
,
and the fluxes
f_{l},f_{r}

are convex.

5.2.2.
(
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
, proof of (
4.10
))

By definition (
4.2
), (
5.1
) of
\mathsf{L}
,

\mathsf{R}
,
it follows that backward characteristics for
u

starting at
(x,T)
,
with
x\in]-\infty,0[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[
,
never crosses the interface
x=0
.
Thus, we recover the Oleǐnik estimates (
4.10
)
as a classical property
of solutions
to conservation laws with strictly convex flux, which
follows from the fact that genuine characteristics never intersect in the interior of the domain
(e.g. see
[
6
, Lemma 3.2]
).

5.2.3.
(
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
, first part of the proof of (
4.13
))

Letting
\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
be the
constant defined as
in (
3.7
) with
f=f_{r}
,
we will prove
the implication

(5.3)

\mathsf{R}\in]0,T\cdot f^{\prime}(B)[\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\omega(\mathsf{R}+)\leq\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}],

assuming

(5.4)

\mathsf{R}\in]0,T\cdot f^{\prime}(B)[\,,\qquad\quad\omega(\mathsf{R}+)>\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\,,

and showing that (
5.4
) leads to a contradiction. To complete the proof of (
4.13
) we will show
in §
5.2.5

that

(5.5)

\mathsf{R}\in]0,T\cdot f^{\prime}(B)[\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\leq\omega(\mathsf{R}-).

The proof of the first implication in (
4.13
) is obtained in entirely similar way.

We divide the proof of (
5.3
) in two steps.
In the first step we construct
the leftmost characteristic curve
\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}
that
starts on the interface
x=0

and reaches the point

(\mathsf{R},T)
, remaining
in the region
\{x>0\}
,
with the property
that all maximal backward characteristics
starting on
\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}
don’t cross the interface
x=0
.
In the second step,
we show that

\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}
is located on the left
of the shock curve
\boldsymbol{x}
constructed as in §
3.4

that emanates from the interface
x=0
and reaches the point

(\mathsf{R},T)
.
Thanks to the assumption (
5.4
)
this leads to a contradiction in accordance
with the characterizing
property of
\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
discussed in Remark
3.3
.

Step 1

Consider the map

\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}:[\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},T]\to[0,+\infty[

defined by setting

(5.6)

\displaystyle\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)

\displaystyle\doteq\inf\big\{R>0\;:\;x-t\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(u{(x,t)})\geq 0\quad\forall\;x\geq R\big\},\quad t\geq 0\,,

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}

\displaystyle\doteq\inf\big\{t\in[0,T]\;:\;\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(s)>0\quad\forall\;s\in[t,T]\big\}\,.

Notice that by definition (
5.6
)
we have

(5.7)

\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}})=0,\qquad\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(T)=\mathsf{R},\qquad\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)>0\quad\ \forall~t\in\,]\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},T],

and that
\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}
is a backward characteristic for
u
starting at
(\mathsf{R},T)
, so that
it holds true
(e.g. see
[
24
]
)

(5.8)

\xi^{\prime}_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)=\begin{cases}f^{\prime}_{r}(u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)\pm,t)\qquad&\text{if}\quad u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)-,t)=u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)+,t),\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\lambda_{r}\big(u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)-,t),\,u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)+,t))\big)\qquad&\text{if}\quad u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)-,t)\neq u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)+,t)\,,\end{cases}

where

(5.9)

\lambda_{r}(u,v)\doteq\frac{f_{r}(v)-f_{r}(u)}{v-u},\qquad u,v\in\mathbb{R},\ u\neq v\,.

We shall provide now a lower bound on the slope of
\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}
.
Let
t_{0}\in\,]\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},T]
, and observe that
by definition (
5.6
)
it follows that
the minimal backward characteristic starting at
(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0}),t_{0})
must cross the interface
x=0

at some non-negative time.
Since such a characteristic is genuine and
has slope
f^{\prime}_{r}(u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})-,t_{0}))\geq 0
,
and because of the

AB
-entropy condition (
2.13
),
it follows that
f_{r}(u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})-,t_{0}))\geq f_{r}(B)

and
u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})-,t_{0})\geq\theta_{r}
.
Hence, it holds true

(5.10)

u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})-,t_{0})\geq B\,.

On the other hand, by definition (
5.6
) we have

(5.11)

f^{\prime}_{r}(u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})+,t_{0}))\leq\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})/t_{0}\,.

Thus, letting
\vartheta_{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0}),+}
denote the maximal backward characteristic
starting at

(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0}),t_{0})
,
because of (
5.11
) it holds true

(5.12)

\vartheta_{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0}),+}(0)=\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})-t_{0}\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})+,t_{0}))\geq 0\,,

and (
5.7
) implies

(5.13)

\displaystyle\vartheta_{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0}),+}(t)

\displaystyle>0\qquad\forall~t\in\,]0,t_{0}]\,.

Moreover,
observe that by
the properties of backward characteristics,
and by definition (
5.6
),
the maximal backward characteristics
\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}

starting at
(\mathsf{R},T)

satisfies

\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)\leq\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}(t)\qquad\forall~t\in[\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},T]\,,

and, in particular, one has

(5.14)

\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})\leq\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}(t_{0})\,.

Since maximal

genuine?

backward characteristics
cannot intersect in the interior of the domain,
it follows from (
5.14
) that

(5.15)

\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})-t_{0}\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})+,t_{0}))=\vartheta_{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0}),+}(0)\leq\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}(0)=\mathsf{R}-T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\omega(\mathsf{R}+))\,.

In turn, (
5.15
)
yields

(5.16)

\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)-\mathsf{R}+T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\omega(\mathsf{R}+))\leq t_{0}\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})+,t_{0}))\,.

Moreover,
one has

(5.17)

\frac{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})-\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}(0)}{t_{0}}\leq\vartheta^{\prime}_{\mathsf{R},+}=f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(\mathsf{R}+))\,.

Since the definition (
4.2
) of
\mathsf{R}
and (
5.4
) imply

f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(\mathsf{R}+))\leq{\mathsf{R}}/{T}<f^{\prime}_{r}(B)
,
we deduce from (
5.17
) that

(5.18)

\frac{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})-\mathsf{R}+T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\omega(\mathsf{R}+))}{t_{0}}<f^{\prime}_{r}(B).

By the monotonicity of
f_{r}^{\prime}
, in turn
the estimates (
5.16
), (
5.18
)
yield

(5.19)

\displaystyle(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\left(\frac{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})-\mathsf{R}+T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\omega(\mathsf{R}+))}{t_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})+,t_{0}),

\displaystyle(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\left(\frac{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})-\mathsf{R}+T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\omega(\mathsf{R}+))}{t_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle<B.

Therefore, recalling (
5.8
), (
5.9
),
and because of the convexity of
f_{r}
,
we derive
from (
5.4
),(
5.10
),
(
5.19
), that

(5.20)

\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}^{\prime}(t_{0})>\lambda_{r}\!\left(\!(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\!\!\left(\frac{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t_{0})-\mathsf{R}+T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}])}{t_{0}}\right)\!,B\!\right)\qquad\forall~t_{0}\in\,]\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},T]\,.

\omega
x
t
\zeta_{\mathsf{R}}{}_{,+}(0)
\zeta_{0}{}_{,}{}_{+}(0)
\tau_{\mathsf{R}}
\xi_{\mathsf{R}}(t)
\mathbf{s}
\mathsf{R}
\mathbf{x}
-\mathsf{L}
