.
Illustration of the proof in §
5.2.3
. The black lines are characteristics of the solution
u
, that cross inside the domain and therefore lead to a contradiction. The blue lines are the comparison curves.

Step 2
(Comparison with an extremal shock).
Let
\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}](\cdot)
be the function defined in
§
3.1

with
f=f_{r}
,
set

(5.21)

\mathsf{L}\doteq\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}](T),

and consider the function

(5.22)

\boldsymbol{x}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}](t),\qquad\quad t\in\big[\boldsymbol{s}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}],T\big]\,,

defined as in §
3.2
,
with
A=\overline{B}
(
\overline{B}
as in (
2.17
)),
and
f=f_{r}
.
By definition (
3.10
),
and applying Lemma
3.1
,
it holds true

(5.23)

\boldsymbol{x}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}](\boldsymbol{s}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}])=0,\qquad\quad\boldsymbol{x}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}](T)=\mathsf{R},

and

(5.24)

\frac{d}{dt}{\boldsymbol{x}}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}](t)=\lambda_{r}\!\left(\!(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\bigg(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}](t)+\mathsf{L}}{t}\bigg),B\,\right),\qquad t\in\big[\boldsymbol{s}[\mathsf{L},\bar{B},f],T\big].

Moreover, because of (
3.7
), (
5.21
), we have

(5.25)

\mathsf{L}=T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}])-\mathsf{R}\,.

Recall that by (
5.7
), (
5.23
), it holds

\xi_{\mathsf{R}}(T)=\mathsf{R}={\boldsymbol{x}}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}](T).

Then, by virtue of (
5.20
), (
5.24
), a comparison argument yields

(5.26)

\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)<\boldsymbol{x}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}](t),\qquad\forall\;t\in\big[\max\big\{\!\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},\,\boldsymbol{s}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}]\big\},\,T\big[\,.

Notice that, if
\boldsymbol{s}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}]\geq\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}
,
then because of (
5.23
),
(
5.26
),
and since
\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)\geq 0
,
for all
t\in[\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},T]
,
we find the contradiction
0\leq\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(\boldsymbol{s}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}])<0
.
Hence it must be

(5.27)

\boldsymbol{s}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}]<\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}.

Next, observe that by definition (
5.6
)
and because of (
5.7
), we have

u(0+,\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}})\leq\theta_{r}
. Thus, by virtue of the

AB
-entropy condition (
2.13
),
it follows that

u(0+,\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}})\leq\overline{B}
.
Then, letting
\zeta_{0,+}:[0,\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}]\to[0,+\infty[

denote
the maximal backward characteristic
starting at
(0,\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}})
, one has

(5.28)

\zeta_{0,+}(0)=-\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(u(0+,\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}))\geq-\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B}).

On the other hand,
by virtue of (
5.4
),
(
5.21
),
(
5.27
),
and recalling the definitions (
3.7
), (
3.9
) of
\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
,
\boldsymbol{s}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}]
, we find that
the maximal backward characteristic

\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}:[0,T]\to[0,+\infty[

from
(\mathsf{R},T)

satisfies

(5.29)

\displaystyle\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}(0)=\mathsf{R}-T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\omega(\mathsf{R}+))

\displaystyle<\mathsf{R}-T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}])

\displaystyle=-\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}](T)

\displaystyle=-\boldsymbol{s}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}]\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})\

\displaystyle<-\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})\,.

Thus, we deduce from (
5.28
)-(
5.29
) that

(5.30)

\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}(0)<\zeta_{0,+}(0),

while (
5.14
) yield

(5.31)

\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}(\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}})>0=\zeta_{0,+}(\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}).

The inequalities (
5.30
)-(
5.31
)
imply that the genuine characteristics

\zeta_{0,+},\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}

intersect each other in the interior of the domain, which gives a contradiction
and thus completes the proof of the implication (
5.3
).

5.2.4.
(
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
, proof of
(
4.15
)-(
4.16
))

We will prove only the implication (
4.16
), the
proof of (
4.15
) being entirely similar.
Let
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\doteq\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\omega,f_{l},f_{r},A,B]

be the constant
in
(
4.4
),
and assume that

(5.32)

\mathsf{R}\in\,]0,T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[\,,\qquad\quad\mathsf{L}<\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,.

Step 1.

(
proof of:
\omega(x)\leq A
in
]\mathsf{L},0[\,
)
.

By definition (
4.2
), (
5.1
) of
\mathsf{L}
,
it follows that backward genuine characteristics starting at points
(x,T)
,
with
x\in\,]\mathsf{L},0[
of continuity for
\omega
, must cross the interface
x=0
at some non-negative time.
Since such characteristics
have slope
f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(x))\leq 0
,
and because of the

AB
-entropy condition (
2.13
),
it follows that
f_{l}(\omega(x))\geq f_{l}(A)

and
\omega(x)\leq\theta_{l}

at any point
x\in\,]\mathsf{L},0[
of continuity for
\omega
.
Hence, we have

\omega(x\pm)\leq A

for all
x\in\,]\mathsf{L},0[\,
.

Step 2.

(
proof of:
\omega(x)=A
in
]\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},0[\,
)
.

In a similar way to (
5.6
), consider
the map

\xi_{{\mathsf{L}}}:[\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}},T]\to]-\infty,0[

defined symmetrically by setting

(5.33)

\displaystyle\xi_{{\mathsf{L}}}(t)

\displaystyle\doteq\sup\big\{L<0\;:\;x-t\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(u{(x,t)})\leq 0\quad\forall\;x\leq L\big\},\quad t\geq 0\,,

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}

\displaystyle\doteq\inf\big\{t\in[0,T]\;:\;\xi_{{\mathsf{L}}}(s)<0\quad\forall\;s\in[t,T]\big\}\,.

Notice that by definition (
5.33
)
we have

\xi_{{\mathsf{L}}}(\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}})=0,\qquad\xi_{{\mathsf{L}}}(T)=\mathsf{L},\qquad\xi_{{\mathsf{L}}}(t)<0\ \quad\forall\;t\in\,]\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}},\,T].

We claim that

(5.34)

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\quad\ \Longrightarrow\ \quad\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\leq\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}],

where
\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
is the constant defined as in (
3.32
), with
f=f_{r}
.
We will prove the implication (
5.34
)
with similar arguments to the proof of (
4.13
) in §
5.2.3
,
assuming

(5.35)

\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\,,\qquad\quad\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}>\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\,,

and showing that (
5.35
)
lead to a contradiction.

Since
\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}
, by definitions (
5.6
), (
5.33
),
and by virtue of the

AB
-entropy condition (
2.13
),
it follows

(5.36)

(u_{l}(t),u_{r}(t))=(A,B)\qquad\forall\;t\in\,]\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},T]\,,

which in turn implies

(5.37)

u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)-,t)=B\qquad\forall\;t\in\,]\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},T]\,.

Let

\zeta_{0,+}
,

\vartheta_{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t),+}
, be
the maximal backward characteristic
starting at
(0,\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}})
, and at

(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t),t)
,

t\in\,]\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},T]
,
respectively. Relying on (
5.12
), (
5.28
),
and since maximal backward characteristics cannot intersect in the interior of the domain, we find

(5.38)

-\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})\leq\zeta_{0,+}(0)\leq\vartheta_{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t),+}(0)=\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)-t\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)+,t))\,.

In turn, (
5.38
)
together with (
5.35
), yields

(5.39)

t\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)+,t))\leq\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)+\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})\qquad\forall~t\in\,]\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},T],

since
f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})<0
.
By the monotonicity of
f^{\prime}_{r}

we deduce from (
5.39
) that

(5.40)

u(\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)+,t)\leq(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\left(\frac{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)+\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})}{t}\right).

Therefore, recalling (
5.8
), (
5.9
),
and because of the convexity of
f_{r}
,
we derive
from (
5.37
), (
5.40
)
that

(5.41)

\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}^{\prime}(t)\leq\lambda_{r}\!\left(\!(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\!\!\left(\frac{\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)+\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})}{t}\right)\!,B\!\right)\qquad\forall~t\in\,]\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},T]\,.

On the other hand, letting
\boldsymbol{x}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}](\cdot)

be the function defined in §
3.2
,
with
\mathsf{L}
as in (
5.21
),

A=\overline{B}
,
and
f=f_{r}
,
we have (
5.23
),
(
5.24
).
Moreover, because of (
3.32
), (
5.21
), it holds true

(5.42)

\mathsf{L}=\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B}),\qquad\boldsymbol{s}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}]=\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\,.

Then, by virtue of (
5.7
), (
5.41
),
and because of (
5.23
), (
5.24
),
(
5.35
),
(
5.42
),
with a comparison argument we deduce

(5.43)

\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(t)\geq\boldsymbol{x}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}](t)\qquad\forall~t\in[\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}},T]\,.

But (
5.43
),
together with (
5.7
),
(
5.35
), (
5.42
),
and recalling (
3.10
),
implies

(5.44)

0=\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}(\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}})\geq\boldsymbol{x}[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}](\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}})>x[\mathsf{L},\overline{B},f_{r}](\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}])=0\,,

which gives a contradiction,
proving the claim (
5.34
).

Relying on the implication (
5.34
), we show now that
\omega(x)=A
in
]\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},0[\,
,
considering two cases:

Case 1:

\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}<\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}
.
Then, by definitions (
5.6
), (
5.33
),
and by virtue of the

AB
-entropy condition (
2.13
),
it follows

(5.45)

(u_{l}(t),u_{r}(t))=(A,B)\qquad\forall\;t\in\,]\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}},T]\,.

Observe that
the maximal backward characteristic

\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},+}

starting at
(\mathsf{L},T)

crosses the interface
x=0

at time
T-\mathsf{L}/f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(\mathsf{L}+)
.
Since
\xi_{{\mathsf{L}}}
is a
backward characteristic
starting at the same point
(\mathsf{L},T)
and
crossing the interface
x=0

at time
\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}
,
one has
\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}\leq T-\mathsf{L}/f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(\mathsf{L}+)
.
This implies that the backward genuine characteristics from
points
(x,T)
,
x\in\,]\mathsf{L},0[
, impact the interface
x=0

at times
t_{x}\geq T-\mathsf{L}/f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(\mathsf{L}+)\geq\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}
. Since the value of the solution
u
is constant along genuine characteristics, we deduce from (
5.45
)
that
\omega(x)=A
for all
x\in\,]\mathsf{L},0[
.
Hence, by (
5.32
) in particular
it follows that

\omega(x)=A

for all

x\in\,]\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},0[\,
.

Case 2:

\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}
.
Then, because of (
5.34
) we have

\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\leq\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
.
Observe that by Step 1 we have
\omega(x)\leq A
for all
x\in\,]\mathsf{L},0[
.
Relying on the monotonicity of
f^{\prime}_{l}
,
this implies that the backward genuine
characteristics starting
from
points
(x,T)
,
x\in\,]\mathsf{L},0[
, impacts the interface
x=0

at times

(5.46)

\tau(x)\doteq T-\frac{x}{f_{l}^{\prime}(\omega(x))}\geq T-\frac{x}{f_{l}^{\prime}(A)}\,.

On the other hand,
recalling definitions (
4.4
),
(
5.1
), we have

(5.47)

T-\frac{x}{f_{l}^{\prime}(A)}\geq T-\frac{(T-\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}])\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(A)}{f_{l}^{\prime}(A)}=\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\geq\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\,,

for all

x\in\,]\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},0[\,
.
Combining (
5.46
), (
5.47
), we deduce that the backward genuine
characteristics starting
from
points
(x,T)
,
x\in\,]\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},0[
, cross the interface
x=0

at times
\tau(x)\geq\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}
.
Hence, relying again on the property that
the solution
u
is constant along genuine characteristics, we
infer from (
5.36
) that
\omega(x)=A

for all

x\in\,]\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},0[\,

also in this case, thus completing the proof of Step 2.

Step 3.

(
proof of:
\omega(\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}-)=A
)
.

We know by Step 1 and Step 2 that

\omega(\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}-)\leq A

and

\omega(\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}+)=A
.
On the other hand the Lax entropy condition (see §
5.2.1
) implies
\omega(\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}-)\geq\omega(\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}+)=A
.
Therefore one has
A\geq\omega(\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}-)\geq\omega(\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}+)=A

which yields
\omega(\tilde{\mathsf{L}}-)=A
.
This concludes the proof of (
4.16
).

5.2.5.
(
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
, proof of (
4.14
) and completion of the proof of (
4.13
))

We will prove only the
second implication in (
4.14
), the proof of the first one being entirely symmetric.
Assume that

(5.48)

\Big[\mathsf{R}\in\,]0,T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)[\,\;\ \mathrm{and}\;\ \widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\leq\mathsf{L}\,\Big]\quad\mathrm{or}\quad\ \mathsf{R}\geq T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B),

and let
\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}},\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}

be the constants defined in (
5.6
), (
5.33
),
in connection with the characteristics
\xi_{{\mathsf{L}}},\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}
.
As observed in Step 2 of §
5.2.4
,
the fact that

\xi_{{\mathsf{L}}}
is a
backward characteristic
starting at
(\mathsf{L},T)

and crossing the interface
x=0
at time

\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}

implies

(5.49)

\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}\leq\,\tau_{+}(\mathsf{L})\doteq T-\frac{\mathsf{L}}{f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(\mathsf{L}+)}\,.

We claim that (
5.48
) implies

(5.50)

\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\leq\,\tau_{+}(\mathsf{L})\,.

Since (
5.49
) clearly implies (
5.50
)
when
\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}
, it will be sufficient to prove the claim under the assumption
\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}<\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}
.
Let’s consider first the case that

(5.51)

\mathsf{R}\in\,]0,T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)[\,\;\ \ \mathrm{and}\ \;\ \widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\leq\mathsf{L}\,.

Observe that,
because of (
5.34
),

\tau_{{\mathsf{L}}}<\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}
implies

(5.52)

\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\leq\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}].

Moreover, by Step 1 of §
5.2.4
, one has
\omega(\mathsf{L}+)\leq A
.
Therefore,
recalling the definition (
4.4
),
and because of the monotonicity
of
f^{\prime}_{l}
, we deduce from

\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\leq\mathsf{L}

that

(5.53)

(T-\boldsymbol{\tau}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}])\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(\mathsf{L}+)\leq\mathsf{L}\,,

which, together with (
5.52
), yields (
5.50
), under the assumption (
5.51
).
Next, consider the case that

(5.54)

\mathsf{R}\geq T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)\,.

Observe that by the analogous argument of Step 1 of §
5.2.4
for (
4.15
), one has
\omega(\mathsf{R}-)\geq B
. Moreover, if
\omega(\mathsf{R}-)=B
,
by definition (
4.2
) of
\mathsf{R}
it follows that

f^{\prime}_{r}(B)\geq\mathsf{R}/T
, which together with (
5.54
), implies

f^{\prime}_{r}(B)=\mathsf{R}/T
.
In turn,
f^{\prime}_{r}(B)=\mathsf{R}/T
implies that the minimal characteristic starting at
(\mathsf{R},T)
reaches the interface
x=0
at time

t=0
, and by definition (
5.6
),
it coincides with
\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}
.
Therefore, one has
\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}=0
,
which proves (
5.50
).
Hence, it remains to consider the case (
5.54
)
when
\omega(\mathsf{R}-)>B
.
Notice that, if

(5.55)

\frac{\mathsf{L}}{f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(\mathsf{L}+))}>\frac{\mathsf{R}}{f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(\mathsf{R}-))}\,,

it follows that the minimal backward characteristic
\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},-}

from
(\mathsf{R},T)
crosses the interface
x=0
at a time

(5.56)

\tau_{-}(\mathsf{R})\doteq T-\frac{\mathsf{R}}{f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(\mathsf{R}-))}

strictly greater than the time

\tau_{+}(\mathsf{L})

at which the maximal
backward characteristic
\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},+}

from
(\mathsf{L},T)
crosses the interface
x=0
.
On the other hand, since
\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},-}
is a genuine characteristic, it follows that

u_{r}(\tau_{-}(\mathsf{R}))=\omega(\mathsf{R}-)>B
.
Because of the
AB
-entropy condition (
2.13
)
this implies that
u_{l}(\tau_{-}(\mathsf{R}))>\theta_{l}
.
Thus we can trace the minimal backward characteristic starting at
(0,\,\tau_{-}(\mathsf{R}))

and lying in
\{x<0\}
, which
has slope
f^{\prime}_{l}(u_{l}(\tau_{-}(\mathsf{R}))>0
, and hence it
will intersect the characteristic
\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},+}
at a positive time
t^{*}\geq\tau_{+}(\mathsf{L})
,
giving a contradiction.
Therefore,
\omega(\mathsf{R}-)>B
implies

(5.57)

\frac{\mathsf{L}}{f^{\prime}_{l}(\omega(\mathsf{L}+))}\leq\frac{\mathsf{R}}{f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(\mathsf{R}-))}\,.

On the other hand,
since
\xi_{{\mathsf{R}}}
is a
backward characteristic
starting at
(\mathsf{R},T)

and crossing the interface
x=0
at time

\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}
,
it holds true

(5.58)

\tau_{{\mathsf{R}}}\leq\tau_{-}(\mathsf{R})\,.

Hence, (
5.56
), (
5.57
), (
5.58
) together yield (
5.50
). This completes the proof of the Claim that (
5.48
) implies (
5.50
).
Then, by definitions (
5.6
), (
5.33
),
relying on (
5.49
), (
5.50
),
and by virtue of the

AB
-entropy condition (
2.13
),
we find that

(5.59)

(u_{l}(t),u_{r}(t))=(A,B)\qquad\forall\;t\in\,]\tau_{+}(\mathsf{L}),\,T]\,.

Since backward genuine characteristics starting
from
points
(x,T)
,
x\in\,]{\mathsf{L}},0[
, cross the interface
x=0

at times
t_{x}\geq\tau_{+}(\mathsf{L})
,
we
infer from (
5.59
) that
\omega(x)=A

for all

x\in\,]{\mathsf{L}},0[\,
. This concludes the proof of the
second implication in (
4.14
).

Concerning
(
4.13
),
we prove now the implication (
5.5
).
To this end observe that,
because of (
4.14
) and (
4.15
) (established in §
5.2.4
), we have

\mathsf{R}\in]0,T\cdot f^{\prime}(B)[\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\omega(x)\geq B\qquad\forall~x\in\,]0,\mathsf{R}[\,,

and hence

(5.60)

\mathsf{R}\in]0,T\cdot f^{\prime}(B)[\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\omega(\mathsf{R}-)\geq B\,.

Thus, relying on (
3.8
)
with
f=f_{r}
,
we deduce (
5.5
) from (
5.60
), which completes the proof of the
second implication in
(
4.13
).

5.2.6.
(
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
, proof of (
4.11
)-(
4.12
))

We will prove only (
4.11
), the proof of (
4.12
) being entirely similar.
Then, assume that (
5.32
) holds as in §
5.2.4
.

Step 1.

For every point
x\in\,]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[
where
\omega

is continuous, consider the map

(5.61)

\vartheta_{x}(t)\doteq\begin{cases}x-(T-t)\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(\omega(x)),&\text{if \ $\tau(x)\leq t\leq T$},\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\cr(t-\tau(x))\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}\circ\pi_{r,-}^{l}(\omega(x)),&\text{if\ $0\leq t<\tau(x)$},\end{cases}

with

(5.62)

\tau(x)\doteq T-\frac{x}{f_{l}^{\prime}(\omega(x))}\,,

and set

(5.63)

\phi(x)\doteq\vartheta_{x}(0)=-\tau(x)\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}\circ\pi_{r,-}^{l}(\omega(x))\,.

Observe that

(5.64)

\displaystyle\vartheta_{x}|_{\,]\tau(x),T]}\quad\text{is a genuine characteristic for u in the halfplane}\ \ \{x<0\}\,,

\displaystyle\vartheta_{x}|_{\,]0,\tau(x)[\,}\quad\text{is a genuine characteristic for u in the halfplane}\ \ \{x>0\}\ \ \text{if}\ \ u_{r}(\tau(x))\leq\overline{B}\,,

and thus
\vartheta_{x}
is a genuine characteristic for
u
as
AB
-solution (see Remark
2.6
) only in the case where
u_{r}(\tau(x))\leq\overline{B}
.
Note also
that
\tau(x)
is the impact time of

\vartheta_{x}
with the interface
x=0
,
and that the function
\tau
has at most countably many discontinuity points as
\omega
.
Since genuine characteristics cannot intersect in the interior of the domain,
it follows that the right continuous extension of
\tau
is a nondecreasing map.
On the other hand,
because
we are assuming that
\omega
satisfies (
H
) and that
(A,B)
is a non critical connection, we know by Proposition
B.3
in Appendix
B
that
no pair of genuine characteristics can meet together on the interface
x=0
. Hence, we deduce that the right continuous extension of
the map
\tau
is actually increasing
on

]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[
.

We will next show that the right continuous extension of the map
\phi

is nondecreasing on
]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[
.

Step 2.

Consider two points

\mathsf{L}<x_{1}<x_{2}<\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}
of continuity for
\omega
. By Step 1 we know that
\tau(x_{1})<\tau(x_{2})
.
Moreover, by (
4.16
) (established in §
5.2.4
) we have
\omega(x)\leq A
for all
x\in\,]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\,
.
Then, we shall provide a proof of

(5.65)

\phi(x_{1})\leq\phi(x_{2})

considering different cases
according to the fact that
\omega(x_{i})=A

or
\omega(x_{i})<A
,
i=1,2
.

Case 1:

\omega(x_{i})<A
,
i=1,2
.
Since
u
is constant along genuine characteristics, and because of the
AB
-entropy condition (
2.13
),
it follows that
u_{r}(\tau(x_{i}))=\pi_{r,-}^{l}(\omega(x_{i}))<\overline{B}
,
i=1,2
.
Therefore, by (
5.64
)
\vartheta_{x_{i}}|_{\,]0,\tau(x_{i})[\,}
,
i=1,2
,
are genuine characteristics
in the half plane
\{x>0\}

starting at
(0,\tau(x_{i}))
,
which cannot intersect at positive times.
This implies
\phi(x_{1})=\vartheta_{x_{1}}(0)\leq\vartheta_{x_{2}}(0)=\phi(x_{2})
.

Case 2:

\omega(x_{i})=A
,
i=1,2
.
By
definition (
5.61
)
we know that
\vartheta_{x_{i}}|_{\,]0,\tau(x_{i})[\,}
,
i=1,2
, are parallel lines
(possibly not characteristics for
u
)
with slope
f^{\prime}_{r}\big(\pi_{r,-}^{l}(A)\big)=f^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{B})
,
starting at
(0,\tau(x_{i}))
.
Hence,
\tau(x_{1})<\tau(x_{2})
implies

\phi(x_{1})=\vartheta_{x_{1}}(0)<\vartheta_{x_{2}}(0)=\phi(x_{2})
.

Case 3:

\omega(x_{1})=A
,
\omega(x_{2})<A
.
Notice that,
by the monotonicity of
f^{\prime}_{l},f^{\prime}_{r}
, the map

\big]-\infty,(f_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}(x_{1}/T)\big]\ni u\mapsto-\left(T-\frac{x_{1}}{f_{l}^{\prime}(u)}\right)\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}\circ\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u)

is decreasing. Then we have

(5.66)

\displaystyle\phi(x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq-\left(T-\frac{x_{1}}{f_{l}^{\prime}(\omega(x_{2}))}\right)\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}\circ\pi^{l}_{r,-}(\omega(x_{2}))

\displaystyle\leq-\left(T-\frac{x_{2}}{f_{l}^{\prime}(\omega(x_{2}))}\right)\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}\circ\pi^{l}_{r,-}(\omega(x_{2}))=\phi(x_{2})\,.

Case 4:

\omega(x_{2})=A
,
\omega(x_{1})<A
. Since
\omega(x_{2})=A
,
it follows with the same arguments as above that
u_{l}(\tau(x_{2}))=A
and that either
u_{r}(\tau(x_{2}))=\overline{B}

or
u_{r}(\tau(x_{2}))=B
. In the first case,
because of (
5.64
)
one can proceed as in Case 1 to deduce that

\phi(x_{1})\leq\phi(x_{2})
. Then, assume
u_{r}(\tau(x_{2}))=B
,
and set (see Figure
16
)

(5.67)

\displaystyle\overline{t}\doteq\inf\Big\{t\leq\tau(x_{2})\;\big|\;(u_{l}(s),u_{r}(s))=(A,B)\quad\forall s\in[t,\tau(x_{2})]\Big\}.

Notice that since
\tau({x_{1}})<\tau({x_{2}})

and because
u_{l}(\tau(x_{1}))<A
implies
u_{r}(\tau(x_{1}))<\overline{B}
, it follows that
\overline{t}\in\,]\tau({x_{1}}),\tau(x_{2})]
. We claim that it must hold

(5.68)

u_{r}(\,\overline{t}\,)=\overline{B}\,.

Towards a proof of (
5.68
), notice first that, since
\omega(x)\leq A
for all
x\in\,]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\,
,
it follows that
u_{l}(t)\leq A
for all

t\in[\tau(x_{1}),\tau(x_{2})]
.

Because of the
AB
-entropy condition (
2.13
)
and by definition of
\overline{t}
,
this implies that there exists a sequence of times
t_{n}\uparrow\overline{t}
such that
u_{r}(t_{n})\leq\overline{B}
.
Then, since
(A,B)
is a non critical connection, we trace the
backward characteristics from points
(0,t_{n})
, with slope
f^{\prime}_{r}(u_{r}(t_{n}))\leq f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})
. Using the stability of characteristics with respect to uniform convergence (see for example the proof of Lemma
C.1
), we thus find that
there is a backward characteristic with slope
\leq f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})
starting from
(0,\overline{t})
. This immediately implies that

(5.69)

u_{r}(\,\overline{t}\,)\leq\overline{B}\,.

Then, consider the blow ups

(5.70)

u_{\rho}(x,t)\doteq u(\rho x,\overline{t}+\rho(t-\overline{t}))\qquad\quad x\in\mathbb{R},\ t\geq 0\,,

of
u
at the point
(0,\overline{t}\,)
, as in the proof of Proposition
B.3
. When
\rho\downarrow 0
, the blow-ups
u_{\rho}(\cdot,t)
converge in
{\bf L^{1}}_{\mathsf{l}oc}
, up to a subsequence, to a limiting
AB
-entropy solution
v(\cdot,t)
,
for all
t>0
.
Moreover, we have

(5.71)

v(x,\overline{t}\,)=\begin{cases}u_{l}(\,\overline{t}\,),&\text{if $x<0$},\\
u_{r}(\,\overline{t}\,),&\text{if $x>0$}.\end{cases}

By definitions (
5.67
), (
5.70
),
it holds true

(5.72)

(u_{\rho,l}(t),u_{\rho,r}(t))=(A,B)\quad\qquad\forall~t\in\Big]\overline{t},\,\overline{t}+\frac{\tau(x_{2})-\overline{t}}{\rho}\Big[\,,

where

u_{\rho,l}(t),u_{\rho,r}(t)
denote the
left and right traces of
u_{\rho}(\cdot,t)
at
x=0
.
Taking the limit as
\rho\downarrow 0

in (
5.72
),
and invoking Corollary
2.11

(with
(A_{n},B_{n})=(A,B)
for all
n
),

we deduce that

(5.73)

v(0-,t)\in\{A,\overline{A}\,\}\qquad\quad v(0+,t)\in\{B,\overline{B}\,\},\qquad\forall\;t>\overline{t},

while (
5.69
), (
5.71
) imply

(5.74)

v(x,\overline{t}\,)=u_{r}(\overline{t}\,)\leq\overline{B},\qquad\forall~x>0\,.

By a direct inspection
we find that, if
an
AB
-entropy solution
of a Riemann problem for (
1.1
) with initial datum (
5.71
)
at time
\overline{t}
, enjoys the properties
(
5.73
)-(
5.74
),
then the initial datum
on
\{(x,\overline{t}\,),\ x>0\}

must be

v(x,\overline{t}\,)=u_{r}(\overline{t}\,)=\overline{B}
, thus proving (
5.68
).

x
t
\omega
\overline{t}
\tau(x_{1})
\tau(x_{2})
x_{1}
x_{2}
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\overline{t}\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})}
\phi(x_{1})
\phi(x_{2})

Figure 16
.
The situation described in Case 4.

Relying on (
5.68
) we can now complete
the proof of (
5.65
).
Since
u_{r}(\tau(x_{1}))<\overline{B}
, we know by (
5.64
) that
\vartheta_{x_{1}}
is a genuine characteristic
in the halfplane
\{x>0\}
starting at
(0,\tau(x_{1}))
.
On the other hand, because of (
5.68
) and since
(A,B)
is a non critical connection, we can trace the maximal backward characteristic from
(0,\overline{t}\,)
in
\{x>0\}
,
which has slope
f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})

and reaches the
x
-axis at the point
-\overline{t}\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{B})
.
Such a (genuine) characteristic cannot intersect at a positive time
the genuine characteristic
\vartheta_{x_{1}}
.
Therefore, one has

(5.75)

\phi(x_{1})=\vartheta_{x_{1}}(0)\leq-\overline{t}\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B}).

Moreover, since
\overline{t}\leq\tau(x_{2})
,
and because
\pi_{r,-}^{l}(\omega(x_{2}))=\pi_{r,-}^{l}(A)=\overline{B}
,
we deduce

-\overline{t}\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})\leq-\tau(x_{2})\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\overline{B})=\phi(x_{2}),

which together with (
5.75
), yields (
5.65
).
This concludes the proof of the nondecreasing monotonicity of
\phi
on

]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[
.
Invoking Lemma 4.4 in
[
6
]
,
this is equivalent to the inequality

D^{+}\omega(x)\leq g[\omega,f_{l},f_{r}](x),\qquad\forall\;x\in\,]\mathsf{L},\tilde{\mathsf{L}}[\,,

where
g
is the function in (
4.6
).
This concludes the proof of (
4.11
), and thus
the proof that
\omega
satisfies conditions (i)-(ii) of Theorem
4.3

is completed.

5.2.7.
(
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}=0
or viceversa, proof of conditions (i)-(ii), or (i)
′
-(ii)
′
, of Theorem
4.11
)

We consider only the case
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}=0
, the other case
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
being symmetrical. The proofs of (
4.46
),
(
4.47
), (
4.48
), (
4.50
), (
4.51
) in this case, are
entirely similar to the proofs of (
4.10
), (
4.11
), (
4.16
), (
4.13
), (
4.17
), respectively,
in the case
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
.
We provide here only the proof
of (
4.49
), which is
the only new constraint arising
in the case
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}=0
,
that was not present
in the case
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
.
Notice first that
by (
4.16
) (established in §
5.2.4
) we know that
\omega(x)\leq A
for all
x\in\,]\mathsf{L},0[\,
.
Hence, since the connection
(A,B)

is non critical, tracing the backward characteristics (with negative slope)
in the half plane
\{x<0\}

from any sequence of points

(x_{n},T)
,
x_{n}\in\,]\mathsf{L},0[\,
,

x_{n}\uparrow 0
,
we deduce that there exists the one-sided limit
u_{l}(T-)

and it holds true

(5.76)

u_{l}(T-)=\omega(0-)\leq A\,.

Then, we will distinguish two cases.

Case 1:
Assume that
u_{r}(t)\geq B
for all
t\in\,]\tau,T[
,
for some
\tau<T
. Then, by the
AB
-entropy condition (
2.13
), and because of (
5.76
),
we deduce that
\omega(0-)=u_{l}(T-)=A
. On the other hand,
since
(A,B)
is a non critical connection,
by definition (
4.2
) it follows that

\mathsf{R}=0
implies
f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(0+))<0
. Therefore we have

\omega(0+)\leq\overline{B}=\pi^{l}_{r,-}(A)=\pi^{l}_{r,-}(\omega(0-))
, proving (
4.49
).

Case 2:

Assume that there exists a sequence of times
t_{n}\uparrow T
such that
u_{r}(t_{n})\leq\overline{B}
for all
n
, and such that

\lim_{n}u_{r}(t_{n})=u^{*}
, for some
u^{*}\leq\overline{B}
.
By the
AB
-entropy condition (
2.13
)
we may also assume
that
u_{r}(t_{n})=\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u_{l}(t_{n}))
for all
n
.
Therefore, relying on (
5.76
), we find

u^{*}=\lim_{n}\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u_{l}(t_{n}))=\pi^{l}_{r,-}(\omega(0-))
.
On the other hand we have

\omega(0+)\leq u^{*}
, since
otherwise
backward genuine characteristics
issuing from points
(x_{n},T)
,

x_{n}\downarrow 0
, would eventually cross backward genuine characteristics
in the half plane
\{x>0\}

starting from
points
(0,t_{n})
.
In fact, if
\omega(0+)>u^{*}

then
we can find points
(x_{n},T)
,

x_{n}>0
(
x_{n}
point of continuity for
\omega
), and

(0,t_{n})
,
t_{n}<T
(
t_{n}
point of continuity for
u_{r}
), such that
\omega(x_{n})>u_{r}(t_{n})
,
which would imply that the backward characteristic
starting from

(x_{n},T)
with negative slope

f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(x_{n},T))

intersect the backward characteristic
starting from
(0,t_{n})

with slope
f^{\prime}_{r}(u_{r}(t_{n}))<f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(x_{n},T))
.
Therefore it must be

\omega(0+)\leq u^{*}
, which
together with
u^{*}=\pi^{l}_{r,-}(\omega(0-))
, yields (
4.49
).

This concludes the proof of (
4.49
), and thus
the proof that
\omega
satisfies conditions (i)-(ii)
(or (i)
′
-(ii)
′
) of Theorem
4.11

is completed.

5.2.8.
(
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}=0
, proof of conditions (i)-(ii) of Theorem
4.14
)

The proofs of (
4.62
),
(
4.64
), are entirely similar to the proofs of (
4.10
),
(
4.13
),
in the case
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
,
and of
(
4.49
),
in the case
\mathsf{L}=\mathsf{R}=0
,
respectively.
Further, (
4.63
)
can be established with the same arguments of
the proof of (
4.13
) in the case
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
,
recalling
Remark
4.15
.
This completes the proof that
\omega
satisfies conditions (i)-(ii) of Theorem
4.14
.

5.3.
Part 1.b -
(1)\Rightarrow(3)
for non critical connections without assuming (
H
)

In this Subsection,
given an element
\omega

of the set
\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)

for a non critical connection

(A,B)
,
we will show that

\omega
satisfies (
H
).
In view of the analysis in §
5.2
,
this will imply that

\omega
fulfills condition (3) of Theorem
4.17
,
thus completing the proof of the implication
(1)\Rightarrow(3)

of Theorem
4.17
.

Then, given
\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)

with

(5.77)

\omega=\mathcal{S}_{T}^{[AB]+}u_{0},\qquad u_{0}\in\mathbf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\,,

set
u(x,t)\doteq S_{t}^{[AB]+}u_{0}(x)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
t\geq 0
.
Next, let
\{u_{n,0}\}_{n}
be a sequence of
functions in
BV(\mathbb{R})

such that

u_{n,0}\ \ \to\ \ u_{0}\quad\text{in}\quad\mathbf{L}^{1}_{\mathsf{l}oc}(\mathbb{R})\,,

and define
u_{n}(x,t)\doteq\mathcal{S}_{t}^{[AB]+}u_{n,0}(x)
.
Then, by Theorem
2.8
-(iii)
it follows

(5.78)

\displaystyle u_{n}(\cdot,t)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\ \ u(\cdot,t)\qquad\text{in}\quad\bf L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R})\hskip 22.99988pt\forall~t\geq 0\,.

Since
(A,B)
is a non critical connection
and because the initial data
u_{n,0}

are in
BV
, invoking the BV bounds on
AB
-entropy solutions
provided in
[
29
, Lemma 8]

(see also

[
1
, Theorem 2.13-(iii)]
),
we deduce that
u_{n}(\cdot,t)\in BV(\mathbb{R})

for all
t>0
, and for all
n
.
Therefore,

u_{n}(\cdot,t)\in\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(t)\,,\quad\text{and \, satisfies~~\eqref{eq:Hhyp}}\quad\forall~t>0\,,\ \ \forall~n\,.

Hence, relying on the analysis in §
5.2
,
and recalling (
4.7
),
we know that,
setting

(5.79)

\mathsf{L}_{n}(t)\doteq\mathsf{L}[u_{n}(\cdot,t),f_{l}]\,,\quad\qquad\mathsf{R}_{n}(t)\doteq\mathsf{R}[u_{n}(\cdot,t),f_{r}]\,,

each

u_{n}(\cdot,t)
satisfies the
conditions stated in:

-

Theorem
4.3
if
\mathsf{L}_{n}(t)<0,\ \mathsf{R}_{n}(t)>0
;

-

Theorem
4.11
if
\mathsf{L}_{n}(t)<0,\ \mathsf{R}_{n}(t)=0
or viceversa;

-

Theorem
4.14
if
\mathsf{L}_{n}(t)=0
,
R_{n}(t)=0
.

Thus, in particular,
u_{n}(\cdot,t)

satisfies the Oleǐnik-type inequalities

(5.80)

\displaystyle D^{+}u_{n}(x,t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t\cdot f^{\prime\prime}_{l}(u_{n}(x,t))}\qquad\quad\text{in}\quad\,]\!-\!\infty,\mathsf{L}_{n}(t)[\,,

\displaystyle D^{+}u_{n}(x,t)

\displaystyle\leq g[u_{n}(\cdot,t),f_{l},f_{r}](x)\quad\ \,\text{in}\quad\,]\mathsf{L}_{n}(t),0[\,,\quad\text{if}\quad\mathsf{L}_{n}(t)<0\,,

\displaystyle D^{+}u_{n}(x,t)

\displaystyle\leq h[u_{n}(\cdot,t),f_{l},f_{r}](x)\quad\ \,\text{in}\quad\,]0,\mathsf{R}_{n}(t)[\,,\quad\text{if}\quad\mathsf{R}_{n}(t)>0\,,

\displaystyle D^{+}u_{n}(x,t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t\cdot f^{\prime\prime}_{r}(u_{n}(x,t))}\qquad\quad\,\text{in}\quad\,]\mathsf{R}_{n}(t),+\infty[\,,

and the constraints

(5.81)

\displaystyle u_{n}(x,t)

\displaystyle\leq A\qquad\forall~x\in\,]\mathsf{L}_{n}(t),0[\,,

\displaystyle u_{n}(x,t)

\displaystyle\geq B\qquad\forall~x\in\,]0,\mathsf{R}_{n}(t)[\,,

for all
t>0
.
Since (
5.81
) implies
f^{\prime}_{r}(u_{n}(x,t))\geq f^{\prime}_{r}(B)

for all
x\in\,]0,\mathsf{R}_{n}(t)[
,
by the monotonicity of
f^{\prime}_{r}
,
we find

(5.82)

\displaystyle t\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(u_{n}(x,t))-x\geq\frac{t\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}{2}\qquad\ \forall~x\in\left[0,\,\min\bigg\{\mathsf{R}_{n}(t),\,\frac{t\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}{2}\bigg\}\right[\,.

Therefore,
recalling definition (
4.6
),
setting

\overline{\Lambda}\doteq\sup_{|z|\leq M}\max\{|f^{\prime}_{l}(z)|,|f^{\prime}_{r}(z)|\}
, with

M
being a uniform
{\bf L^{\infty}}
bound
for
u_{n}
,
and letting
a
be the lower bound on
f^{\prime\prime}_{l},f^{\prime\prime}_{r}

given in (
1.4
),
we deduce from (
5.82
),
that,
if

\mathsf{R}_{n}(t)\leq\frac{t\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}{2}\,,

then for all
n
it holds true

(5.83)

\qquad h[u_{n}(\cdot,t),f_{l},f_{r}](x)\leq\dfrac{{\overline{\Lambda}}^{\,2}}{a\,f^{\prime}_{r}(B)\big(t\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(u_{n}(x,t))-x\big)}\leq\dfrac{2}{a\,t}\cdot\bigg(\dfrac{\overline{\Lambda}}{f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}\bigg)^{\!2}\qquad\forall~x\in[0,\mathsf{R}_{n}(t)[\,,

while if

\mathsf{R}_{n}(t)>\frac{t\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}{2}\,,

then for all
n
it holds true

(5.84)

h[u_{n}(\cdot,t),f_{l},f_{r}](x)\leq\begin{cases}\dfrac{{\overline{\Lambda}}^{\,2}}{a\,f^{\prime}_{r}(B)\big(t\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(u_{n}(x,t))-x\big)}\leq\dfrac{2}{a\,t}\cdot\bigg(\dfrac{\overline{\Lambda}}{f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}\bigg)^{\!2}\qquad\forall~x\in\left[0,\,\frac{t\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}{2}\right]\,,\\
\vskip 12.0pt plus 4.0pt minus 4.0pt\cr\dfrac{\overline{\Lambda}}{x\,a}~\leq~\dfrac{2\,\overline{\Lambda}}{a\,t\!\cdot\!f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}\qquad\qquad\forall~x\in\left[\frac{t\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}{2},\,\mathsf{R}_{n}(t)\right[\,.\end{cases}

Hence,
we derive from (
5.80
), (
5.83
), (
5.84
), the uniform bounds

(5.85)

\displaystyle D^{+}u_{n}(x,t)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{2\,\overline{\Lambda}}{a\,t\!\cdot\!f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}\cdot\max\bigg\{1,\,\dfrac{\overline{\Lambda}}{f^{\prime}_{r}(B)}\bigg\}\quad\text{in}\quad\,]0,\mathsf{R}_{n}(t)[\,,\qquad\text{if}\quad\mathsf{R}_{n}(t)>0\,,

\displaystyle D^{+}u_{n}(x,t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t\cdot a}\quad\text{in}\quad\,]\mathsf{R}_{n}(t),+\infty[\,,

for all n.
Since
(A,B)
is a non critical connection, for every fixed
\delta>0
,
the one-sided uniform upper bounds
provided by (
5.85
)
yield uniform bounds
on the total increasing variation (and hence on the total variation as well) of
u_{n}(t)
,
t\geq\delta
, on bounded subsets
of
[0,+\infty[
.
Thus, by the lower-semicontinuity of the total variation with respect to the
\bf L^{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{loc}}}
convergence,
and because of (
5.78
),
we find that

(5.86)

u(\cdot,t)\in BV_{\mathrm{loc}}([0,+\infty[\,),\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\forall~t\geq\delta}\,.

With the same type of arguments, relying on (
5.80
), (
5.81
), we can show that

(5.87)

u(\cdot,t)\in BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\,]\!-\infty,0]),\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\forall~t\geq\delta}\,.

Therefore, we deduce from
(
5.86
),
(
5.87
), that

(5.88)

u(\cdot,t)\in BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R})\qquad\forall~t>0\,,

which shows that the function
\omega
in (
5.77
)
satisfies condition (
H
), thus completing the proof of
the implication
(1)\Rightarrow(3)
of Theorem
4.17
in the case of a non critical connection.

5.4.
Part 2.a -
(3)\Rightarrow(1)
for non critical connections.

In this Subsection, given

(5.89)

\omega\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}},\qquad\quad\mathsf{L}\doteq\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]<0,\quad\mathsf{R}\doteq\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}]>0\,,

(
\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}
being the set in (
4.8
)),
assuming that

(5.90)

\omega\quad\text{satisfies conditions (i)-(ii) of Theorem \ref{thm:attprofiles}},

we will
show that
\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{AB}(T)
by explicitly constructing an
AB
-entropy solution attaining
\omega
at time
T
.
With entirely similar arguments one can show that
the same conclusion hold assuming that
\omega\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}
:

-

satisfies the conditions of Theorem
4.11
, if
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
or viceversa;

-

satisfies the conditions of Theorem
4.14
, if
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}=0
.

Then, consider
\omega
satisfying (
5.89
),
(
5.90
).
By Remark
4.5

we can distinguish six cases
of pointwise constraints on
\omega
, prescribed by condition (ii)
of Theorem
4.3
,
which depend on the reciprocal positions of the points

\mathsf{L}
,
\mathsf{R}
,
and

\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}
,

\widetilde{\mathsf{R}}
, defined in (
4.2
)-(
4.4
).
We shall consider here only the
Cases 1
and
2
discussed in Remark
4.5
. The
Cases 1b, 2b
are symmetrical to
Cases 1, 2
, up to a change of variables
x\mapsto-x
,
while the
Cases 3, 4
are entirely similar or simpler.

Notice that, by Remark
4.5
,
in
Case 1
it holds true (
4.27
), (
4.28
),
and in particular we shall assume that

(5.91)

\omega(\mathsf{R}+)<\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\,,

while in
Case 2
it holds true (
4.28
), (
4.29
),
and we shall assume that (
5.91
) is
verified together with

(5.92)

\omega(\mathsf{L}-)>\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]\,.

The cases in which
\omega(\mathsf{R}+)=\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
or
\omega(\mathsf{L}-)=\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]
can be treated with entirely similar or simpler arguments.
Moreover, in both
Cases 1
and
2
we have

(5.93)

\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}>\mathsf{L}\,,\qquad\quad\omega(\,\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}-)=\omega(\,\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}+)\,.

The construction of the initial datum
u_{0}

so that the corresponding
AB
-entropy solution solution
u(x,t)\doteq\mathcal{S}_{t}^{[AB]+}u_{0}(x)
attains the value
\omega

at time
T
follows a by now standard procedure (see
[
6
]
,
[
8
]
), that we describe in §
5.4.4
-
5.4.5
below. To this end we first introduce some technical notations
in §
5.4.1
-
5.4.3
.

5.4.1.
Characteristics of compression waves

We introduce a class of
curves connecting two points

(z,0)
,
(y,T)
,
that will be treated as characteristics
of compression waves
generating a shock
at the point
(y,T)
.
In particular, in the case
y<0<z
,
such curves will be
characteristics of a compression wave
that starts at time
t=0
on the half plane
\{z\geq 0\}
,
and generates a shock
at time
t=T

after being refracted at
the discontinuity interface.
Given any
y<0
,
consider the continuous function

]-\infty,(f_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}(y/T)]\ni u\mapsto h_{y}(u)\doteq-\Big(T-\frac{y}{f_{l}^{\prime}(u)}\Big)\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}\circ\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u).

Notice that, by definition (
4.1
) and since
f^{\prime}_{l},f^{\prime}_{r}
are increasing functions,
it follows that
u\mapsto-(T-y/f^{\prime}_{l}(u))
,
u\mapsto f_{r}^{\prime}\circ\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u)
are decreasing maps, and hence the map
h_{y}
is decreasing as well. On the other hand we have

\displaystyle{\lim_{u\to-\infty}}h_{y}(u)=+\infty
,
h_{y}((f_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}(y/T)-)=0
.
Therefore by a continuity and monotonicity argument, it follows that,
for every
z>0
, there exists a unique state
u_{y,z}\leq(f_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}(y/T)
, such that

(5.94)

h_{y}(u_{y,z})=z\,.

Moreover, the map
z\mapsto u_{y,z}
,
z>0

is continuous.
Then, for every pair

y<0<z
,
we denote by
\eta_{y,z}:[0,T]\mapsto\mathbb{R}
the polygonal line given by

(5.95)

\eta_{y,z}(t)\doteq\begin{cases}y-(T-t)\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(u_{y,z}),&\ \ \text{if \ $\tau(y,z)<t\leq T$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr(t-\tau({y,z}))\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}\circ\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u_{y,z}),&\ \ \text{if \ $0\leq t\leq\tau(y,z)$},\end{cases}

where

(5.96)

\tau(y,z)\doteq T-\frac{y}{f_{l}^{\prime}(u_{y,z})}.

Next
, for every pair

y,z<0
, or
y,z>0
, we
denote by
\eta_{y,z}:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}
the segment

(5.97)

\eta_{y,z}(t)\doteq y-(T-t)\cdot\dfrac{(y-z)}{T}\qquad\ \forall~0\leq t\leq T\,.

Notice that,
in the case
y<0<z
,
if we consider a function
u(x,t)
that
assumes the values

\displaystyle u=u_{y,z}

\displaystyle\text{on the segment}\quad\eta_{y,z}(t),\ \tau(y,z)<t\leq T,

\displaystyle u=\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u_{y,z})

\displaystyle\text{on the segment}\quad\eta_{y,z}(t),\ 0\leq t\leq\tau(y,z),

then the states
u_{l}=u_{y,z}
,
u_{r}=\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u_{y,z})
satisfy the interface entropy condition (
2.13
)
at time
t=\tau(y,z)
, and
\eta_{y,z}

enjoys the properties of a (genuine) characteristic for
u
as
an
AB
-entropy solution
(see Remark
2.6
).
Similar observations hold for
\eta_{y,z}

in the case
y,z<0
, considering a function

u(x,t)
that
assumes the value
(f^{\prime}_{l})^{-1}((y-z)/T)=u_{y,z}

along the segment
\eta_{y,z}
,
and in the case
y,z>0
, considering a function

u(x,t)
that
assumes the value
(f^{\prime}_{r})^{-1}((y-z)/T)=u_{y,z}

along the segment
\eta_{y,z}
.

5.4.2.
Maximal/minimal backward characteristics

We introduce a class of
curves with end point
(y,T)

that will be treated as
maximal and minimal
backward characteristics
starting at
(y,T)
.
For every
y\in\,]-\infty,\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,]\,\cup\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[
, we denote by
\vartheta_{y,\pm}:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}
the segments or polygonal
lines

(5.98)

\vartheta_{y,\pm}(t)\doteq\!\begin{cases}y-(T-t)\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}\big(\omega(y\pm)\big),&\text{ if \ $y<\mathsf{L},\quad 0\leq t\leq T$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr y-(T-t)\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}\big(\omega(y\pm)\big),&\text{ if \ $\mathsf{L}\leq y\leq\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},\quad\tau_{\pm}(y)\leq t\leq T$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\big(t-\tau_{\pm}(y)\big)\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}\circ\pi^{l}_{r,-}(\omega(y\pm)),&\text{ if \ $\mathsf{L}\leq y\leq\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},\quad 0\leq t<\tau_{\pm}(y),$}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr y-(T-t)\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}\big(\omega(y\pm)\big),&\text{ if \ $y>\mathsf{R},\quad 0\leq t\leq T$},\end{cases}

where

(5.99)

\tau_{\pm}(y)\doteq T-\frac{y}{f_{l}^{\prime}(\omega(y\pm))}.

We will write
\vartheta_{y}(t)\doteq\vartheta_{y,\pm}(t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
, whenever
\omega(y-)=\omega(y+)
.
In particular, because o
f
(
5.93
), we have
\vartheta_{\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}}(t)\doteq\vartheta_{\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},\pm}(t)
.

Further
, for
y=\mathsf{R}
, we denote
by
\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}
the segment

(5.100)

\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}(t)\doteq\mathsf{R}-(T-t)\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}\big(\omega(\mathsf{R}+)\big)\qquad\forall~0\leq t\leq T\,.

Notice that, because of definition (
4.2
), (
5.89
), whenever
y\in\,]-\infty,{\mathsf{L}}\,[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[
, the curves
\vartheta_{y,\pm}
are segments that never cross the interface

\{x=0\}
,
instead for all
y\in\,]\mathsf{L},\,\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}]
,
\vartheta_{y,\pm}

are polygonal lines that are refracted
at
\{x=0\}
.
Moreover, at every point of discontinity
y\in\,]-\infty,{\mathsf{L}}\,[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[
of
\omega
, conditions (
4.10
), (
4.11
)
imply the Lax condition
\omega(y-)>\omega(y+)
, which in turn,
by the monotonicity of
f^{\prime}_{l},f^{\prime}_{r}
, implies

(5.101)

\vartheta_{y,-}(0)<\vartheta_{y,+}(0)\qquad\quad\forall~y\in\,]-\infty,{\mathsf{L}}\,[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[\,.

As in §
5.4.1
,
observe that in the case
\mathsf{L}<y\leq\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}
,
if we consider a function
u(x,t)
that
assumes the values

\displaystyle u=\omega(y\pm)

\displaystyle\text{on the segment}\quad\vartheta_{y,\pm}(t),\ \tau_{\pm}(y)<t\leq T,

\displaystyle u=\pi^{l}_{r,-}(\omega(y\pm))

\displaystyle\text{on the segment}\quad\vartheta_{y,\pm}(t),\ 0\leq t\leq\tau_{\pm}(y),

than
\vartheta_{y,\pm}

enjoys the properties of a maximal/minimal backward characteristic for
u
as
an
AB
-entropy solution that attains the value
\omega
at time
T
.
Similar observations hold for
\vartheta_{y,\pm}

in the case
y<\mathsf{L}
or
y\geq\mathsf{R}
,
considering a function
u(x,t)
that
assumes the value
\omega(y\pm)
along
\vartheta_{y,\pm}
.

5.4.3.
Partition of
\mathbb{R}

The initial datum will be defined in a different way on different intervals of
the following partition of
\mathbb{R}
(see Figure
18
):

(5.102)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{{\mathsf{L}}}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{x\in\mathbb{R}\;\big|\;\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},-}(0)<x<\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},+}(0)\Big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{{\mathsf{R}}}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{x\in\mathbb{R}\;\big|\;-\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}](T)<x<\mathsf{R}-T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\omega(\mathsf{R}+))\Big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{R}})\;\big|\;\nexists\;y\in\mathbb{R}\;:\;\vartheta_{y,+}(0)=x\;\text{or}\;\vartheta_{y,-}(0)=x\Big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}a}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{R}})\;\big|\;\exists\;y<z\;:\;\vartheta_{y,+}(0)=\vartheta_{z,-}(0)=x\Big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{W}}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{R}})\;\big|\;\exists!\;y\in\mathbb{R}\;:\;\vartheta_{y,+}(0)=x\;\text{or}\;\vartheta_{y,-}(0)=x\Big\},

where
\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}](T)

is defined as in §
3.1

with
f=f_{r}
. Notice that the set
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{R}}
is non empty because
the increasing monotonicity of
f^{\prime}_{r}
,
together with (
3.7
), (
5.91
),
implies

f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(\mathsf{R}+))<f^{\prime}_{r}\big(\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\big)=\frac{\mathsf{R}+\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}](T)}{T}\,.

x
\mathsf{L}
\mathsf{R}
A
B
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}}
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{R}}
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{C}}
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{W}}

.

.

.
Partition of
\mathbb{R}

{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathsf{L}}\cup{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathsf{R}}\cup{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{C}}\cup{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{W}}
.

The elements of this partition
enjoy the following properties.

-

In the
Case 1
, the set
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}}
consists of the starting points of a compression wave
that is partly refracted by the interface, and that generates a shock at the point
(\mathsf{L},T)
.
In the
Case 2
, only the subsets of
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}}

given by
\,]\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},-}(0),\,-\boldsymbol{\sigma}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(\overline{A})[
,   and
](\mathsf{L}/f^{\prime}_{l}(A)-T)\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{B}),\,\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},+}(0)[
,
consist of the starting points of compression waves with center at the point
(\mathsf{L},T)
.
In the complementary sets of
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}}
:

]-\boldsymbol{\sigma}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(\overline{A}),\,0[
and
]0,\,(\mathsf{L}/f^{\prime}_{l}(A)-T)\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{B})[
,
the initial datum will assume the constant values
\overline{A}
and
\overline{B}
, respectively.
Here
\boldsymbol{\sigma}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]
is the
constant defined as in §
3.5
,
with
f=f_{l}
.

-

The set
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{R}}
consists of the starting points of a compression wave that generates a shock at the point
(\mathsf{R},T)
.

-

The set
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{C}}
consists of the
starting points of compression
waves that generate a shock at points
(y,T)
,

y\in\,]-\infty,\mathsf{L}[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[\,
.
The set
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{C}}
is a disjoint union of at most countably many open intervals
of the form

(5.103)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\mathsf{L}}

\displaystyle=\,]x_{n}^{-},x_{n}^{+}[\,,\quad x_{n}^{\pm}=\vartheta_{y_{n},\pm}(0),\qquad y_{n}\in\,]-\infty,\mathsf{L}[\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\mathsf{R}}

\displaystyle=\,]x_{n}^{-},x_{n}^{+}[\,,\quad x_{n}^{\pm}=\vartheta_{y_{n},\pm}(0),\qquad y_{n}\in\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{\mathsf{L}}

\displaystyle=\,]x_{n}^{-},x_{n}^{+}[\,,\quad x_{n}^{\pm}=\vartheta_{y_{n},\pm}(0),\qquad y_{n}\in\,]\mathsf{L},\tilde{\mathsf{L}}[\,,

which are non empty because of (
5.101
).

-

The set
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}a}
consists of at most countably many points that are the
centers of rarefaction waves originated at time
t=0
.

-

The set
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{W}}

consists of the
starting points of all genuine characteristics reaching points
(y,T)
,

y\in\,]-\infty,\mathsf{L}[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[\,
.

5.4.4.
Construction of
AB
-entropy solution on two regions with vertexes
at
(\mathsf{L},T)
and at
({\mathsf{R}},T)

Consider the two polygonal regions

(5.104)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times[0,T]\;:\;\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},-}(t)<x<\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},+}(t)\Big\},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times[0,T]\;:\;\vartheta_{\,\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}}(t)<x<\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}(t)\Big\}.

In the
Case 2

(see Figure
7
),
letting
\Delta[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]
be the region defined as in §
3.5
, with
f=f_{l}
,
we can express
\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}
as

(5.105)

\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}=\Delta[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]\,\cup\,\bigcup_{i=1}^{4}\Delta_{\mathsf{L},i},

where

(5.106)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathsf{L},1}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{(x,t)\in\,]-\infty,0[\,\times[0,T]\;:\;\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},-}(t)<x\leq\mathsf{L}-(T-t)\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}])\Big\},

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathsf{L},2}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{(x,t)\in\,]-\infty,0]\,\times\,[0,T]\;:\;x\geq(t-\boldsymbol{\sigma}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}])\cdot f^{\prime}_{l}(\,\overline{A}\,)\Big\},

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathsf{L},3}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{(x,t)\in\,]0,+\infty[\,\times\,[0,T]\;:\;x<\eta_{\mathsf{L},\,x({A,\overline{B}})}(t)\Big\},

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathsf{L},4}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\,\times\,[0,T]\;:\;\eta_{\mathsf{L},\,x({A,\overline{B}})}(t)\leq x<\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},+}(t)\Big\},

with
\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]
as in (
3.11
) taking
f=f_{l}
,
and

(5.107)

x({A,\overline{B}})\doteq\big(\mathsf{L}/f^{\prime}_{l}(A)-T\big)\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{B})>0\,.

Similarly, in both
Cases 1, 2

(see Figures
6
-
7
),
letting
\Gamma[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
, be the region defined as in §
3.4
, with
f=f_{r}
,
we can express
\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}
as

(5.108)

\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}=\Gamma[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\,\cup\,\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}\Gamma_{\mathsf{R},i},

where

(5.109)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathsf{R},1}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{(x,t)\in\,]-\infty,0]\,\times\,[0,T]\;:\;\vartheta_{\,\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}}(t)<x\Big\},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathsf{R},2}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{(x,t)\in\,]0,+\infty[\,\times\,[0,T]\;:\;x\leq\mathsf{R}-(T-t)\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(B)\Big\},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathsf{R},3}

\displaystyle\doteq\Big\{(x,t)\in\,]0,+\infty[\,\times\,[0,T]\,\;:\;\mathsf{R}-(T-t)\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}])\leq x<\vartheta_{\mathsf{R},+}(t)\Big\},

with
\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{l}]
as in (
3.7
), taking
f=f_{r}
.

Now, consider the function
u_{\mathsf{L}}:\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}\to\mathbb{R}
defined by
setting for every
(x,t)\in\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}
:

in
Case 1
:

(5.110)

u_{\mathsf{L}}(x,t)=\begin{cases}(f_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\frac{\mathsf{L}-x}{T-t}\big),&\text{if $x\leq 0$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u_{\mathsf{L},z}),&\text{if $x=\eta_{\mathsf{L},z}(t)$, \ for some \ $z>0$},\end{cases}

where
u_{\mathsf{L},z}
is defined as in §
5.4.1
, with
y=\mathsf{L}
;

in
Case 2
:

(5.111)

u_{\mathsf{L}}(x,t)=\begin{cases}(f_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\frac{\mathsf{L}-x}{T-t}\big),&\text{if
$(x,t)\in\Delta_{\mathsf{L},1}\cup\Delta_{\mathsf{L},4},\ \ x\leq 0,$}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u_{\mathsf{L},z}),&\text{if $(x,t)\in\Delta_{\mathsf{L},4}$, \ $x=\eta_{\mathsf{L},z}(t)$, \ for some \ $z>0$ }\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\mathsf{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}](x,t),&\text{if $(x,t)\in\Delta[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}],$}\\
\overline{A},&\text{if $(x,t)\in\Delta_{\mathsf{L},2}$,}\\
\overline{B},&\text{if $(x,t)\in\Delta_{\mathsf{L},3}$.}\\
\end{cases}

where
\mathsf{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]
denotes the function defined in (
3.42
), with
f=f_{l}
.

By construction, because of (
1.4
), and relying on the analysis in
3.5
, it follows that
in both
Cases 1, 2
, the function
u_{\mathsf{L}}(x,t)
:

-

is locally Lipschitz continuous on

\big(\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}\setminus\overline{\Delta[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]}\,\big)\cap\big((\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\})\,\times\,]0,T[\,\big)
, and it is continuous on the boundary
\partial\Delta[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]\setminus\big(\{0\}\,\times\,]0,T[\big)
;

-

is a classical solution of
u_{t}+f_{l}(u)_{x}=0
on

\big(\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}\setminus\overline{\Delta[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]}\,\big)\cap\big(\,]-\infty,0[\,\times\,]0,T[\,\big)
, and of
u_{t}+f_{r}(u)_{x}=0
on

\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}\cap\big(\,]0,+\infty[\,\times\,]0,T[\,\big)
;

-

is an entropy weak solution of
u_{t}+f_{l}(u)_{x}=0
on

\Delta[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]
;

-

satisfies
the interface entropy condition (
2.13
)
at any point
(0,t)
,
t\leq\tau_{+}(\mathsf{L})
.

Therefore, by Definition
2.2
,
we deduce that
u_{\mathsf{L}}
is an
AB
-entropy solution of (
1.1
) on
\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}
.

Next, consider (for both
Cases 1, 2
) the function
u_{\mathsf{R}}:\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}\to\mathbb{R}
defined by
setting for every
(x,t)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}
:

(5.112)

u_{\mathsf{R}}(x,t)=\begin{cases}A,&\text{if $(x,t)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{R},1},$}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr B,&\text{if $(x,t)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{R},2},$}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\mathsf{u}[\mathsf{R},A,f_{r}](x,t),&\text{if $(x,t)\in\Gamma[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\frac{\mathsf{R}-x}{T-t}\big),&\text{if $(x,t)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{R},3},$}\end{cases}

where
\mathsf{u}[\mathsf{R},A,f_{r}]
denotes the function defined in (
3.35
), with
f=f_{r}
.
By construction and relying on the analysis in
§
3.4
,
we deduce as above that
u_{\mathsf{R}}

provides an
AB
-entropy solution of (
1.1
)
on
\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}
.
Moreover, because of (
4.27
), (
4.28
), (
4.29
),
we have

(5.113)

u_{\mathsf{R}}(x,T)=\omega(x)\qquad\forall~x\in\,]\widetilde{\mathsf{L}},\mathsf{R}[\,.

5.4.5.
Construction of

AB
-entropy solution on whole
\mathbb{R}\times[0,T]

Observing that, because of (
5.102
), (
5.104
), we have
\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}\cap\{x=0\}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}}
,
\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}\cap\{x=0\}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{R}}
,
we define the initial datum on
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{R}}
as

(5.114)

u_{0}(x)=\begin{cases}u_{\mathsf{L}}(x,0)\ \ &\text{if}\quad x\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr u_{\mathsf{R}}(x,0)\ \ &\text{if}\quad x\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{R}}.\end{cases}

where, in
Case 1
,

(5.115)

u_{\mathsf{L}}(x,0)=\begin{cases}(f_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\frac{\mathsf{L}-x}{T}\big),&\text{if $x\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}},$ \ $x\leq 0$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u_{\mathsf{L},z}),&\text{if $x\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}}$, \ $x=\eta_{\mathsf{L},z}(0)$, \ for some \ $z>0$},\end{cases}

while, in
Case 2
,

(5.116)

u_{\mathsf{L}}(x,0)=\begin{cases}(f_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\frac{\mathsf{L}-x}{T}\big),&\text{if \ $x\in\,]\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},-}(0),\,\mathsf{L}-T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}])[$,}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\overline{A},&\text{if \ $x\in\,]\mathsf{L}-T\cdot f_{l}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{v}[\mathsf{L},A,f_{l}]),\,0[\,$,}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\overline{B},&\text{if \ $x\in\,]0,\,\eta_{\mathsf{L},\,x({A,\overline{B}})}(0)[$,}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u_{\mathsf{L},z}),&\text{if \ $x\in[\eta_{\mathsf{L},\,x({A,\overline{B}})}(0),\,\vartheta_{\mathsf{L},+}(0)[\,$, \ $x=\eta_{\mathsf{L},z}(t)$, \ for some \ $z>0$,}\end{cases}

and, in both
Cases 1, 2
,

(5.117)

u_{\mathsf{R}}(x,0)=(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\textstyle{\frac{\mathsf{R}-x}{T}}\big),\quad\text{if \ $x\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{R}}.$}

In view of the observations in §
5.4.1
-
5.4.2
,
the construction of the
AB
-entropy solution
on

\big(\mathbb{R}\times[0,T]\big)\setminus\big(\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}\big)
, and the corresponding definition of the initial datum on
\mathbb{R}\setminus\big(\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{R}}\big)
, proceed as follows:

-

For any
y\in\,]-\infty,\mathsf{L}[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[\,
, we trace the lines
\vartheta_{y,\pm}
starting at
(y,T)
until they reach the
x
-axis at the point
\phi_{\pm}(y)\doteq\vartheta_{y,\pm}(0)
.
Since conditions (
4.10
), (
4.11
) of Theorem
4.3
is equivalent to the monotonicity of the map
\phi(y)\doteq\vartheta_{y}(0)
(see
[
6
, Lemma 4.4]
), it follows that
\vartheta_{y,\pm}
never intersect each other in the region

\mathbb{R}\times\,]0,T[\,
.
Then, if
y\in\,]-\infty,\mathsf{L}[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[\,
, we define a function
u(x,t)
that is
equal to
\omega(y\pm)
along the segment
\vartheta_{y,\pm}
. Instead if

y\in\,]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\,

we define
u
to be equal to
\omega(y\pm)

along the segment
\vartheta_{y,\pm}(t)
,
\tau_{\pm}(y)\leq t\leq T
, and to be equal
to
\pi^{l}_{r,-}(\omega(y\pm))
along the segment
\vartheta_{y,\pm}(t)
,
0\leq t\leq\tau_{\pm}(y)
.

-

For any
z\in\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\mathsf{L}}\,\cup\,\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\mathsf{R}}
, we trace the line
\eta_{y_{n},z}
,
y_{n}\in\,]-\infty,\mathsf{L}[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[\,
.
By construction the lines
\eta_{y_{n},z}
never cross each other in the region

\mathbb{R}\times\,]0,T[\,
.
Then, if
y_{n}\in\,]-\infty,\mathsf{L}[
, we define
u(x,t)
to be equal to

(f^{\prime}_{l})^{-1}((y-z)/T)=u_{y,z}
along the segment
\eta_{y_{n},z}
, instead if
y_{n}\in\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[
, we define
u(x,t)
to be equal to

(f^{\prime}_{r})^{-1}((y-z)/T)=u_{y,z}
along the segment
\eta_{y_{n},z}
.

-

For any
z\in\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{\mathsf{L}}
, we trace the polygonal line
\eta_{y_{n},z}
,
y_{n}\in\,]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\,
. By construction the lines
\eta_{y_{n},z}
never cross each other in the region

\mathbb{R}\times\,]0,T[\,
.
Then, we define
u(x,t)
to be equal to

(f^{\prime}_{l})^{-1}((y_{n}-x)/(T-t))=u_{y,z}
along the segment
\eta_{y_{n},z}
,

\tau(y,z)<t\leq T
, and to be equal to

\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u_{y,z})
along the segment

\eta_{y_{n},z}
,

0\leq t\leq\tau(y,z)
.

Therefore, we define the
the function

(5.118)

u(x,t)\doteq\begin{cases}\omega(y\pm),&\text{if \ $x=\vartheta_{y,\pm}(t)$ \ for some \ $y\in\,]-\infty,\mathsf{L}[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[\,$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\omega(y\pm),&\text{if \ $x=\vartheta_{y,\pm}(t)<0$ \ for some \ $y\in\,]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\,$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\pi^{l}_{r,-}(\omega(y\pm)),&\text{if \ $x=\vartheta_{y,\pm}(t)>0$ \ for some \ $y\in\,]\mathsf{L},\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}[\,$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr(f_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\frac{y_{n}-z}{T}\big),&\text{if \ $x=\eta_{y_{n},z}(t)$ \ for some \ $z\in\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\mathsf{L}}$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\frac{y_{n}-z}{T}\big),&\text{if \ $x=\eta_{y_{n},z}(t)$ \ for some \ $z\in\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\mathsf{R}}$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr(f_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\frac{\mathsf{L}-x}{T-t}\big),&\text{if \ $x=\eta_{y_{n},z}(t)<0$ \ for some \ $z\in\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{\mathsf{L}}$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u_{y_{n},z}),&\text{if \ $x=\eta_{y_{n},z}(t)>0$ \ for some \ $z\in\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{\mathsf{L}}$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr u_{\mathsf{L}}(x,t),&\text{if \ $(t,x)\in\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr u_{\mathsf{R}}(x,t),&\text{if \ $(t,x)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}$},\end{cases}

and the initial datum

(5.119)

u_{0}(x)\doteq\begin{cases}\omega(y\pm),&\text{if \ $x\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{W}}$, $x=\theta_{y,\pm}(0)$ \ for some \ $y\in\,]-\infty,\mathsf{L}[\,\cup\,]\mathsf{R},+\infty[\,$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\pi^{l}_{r,-}(\omega(y\pm)),&\text{if \ $x\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{W}}$, $x=\theta_{y,\pm}(0)$, $y\in\,]\mathsf{L},\tilde{\mathsf{L}}[\,$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr(f_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\frac{y_{n}-x}{T}\big),&\text{if \ $x\in\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\mathsf{L}}$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\frac{y_{n}-x}{T}\big),&\text{if \ $x\in\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\mathsf{R}}$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\pi^{l}_{r,-}(u_{y_{n},x}),&\text{if \ $x\in\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{\mathsf{L}}$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr u_{\mathsf{L}}(x,0),&\text{if \ $x\in\mathcal{I}_{{\mathsf{L}}}$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr u_{\mathsf{R}}(x,0),&\text{if \ $x\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{R}}$}.\end{cases}

Notice that
u_{0}
is not defined on the countable set
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}a}
which is of measure zero, and clearly
u_{0}\in{\bf L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.
By construction, the function
u(x,t)
:

-

is locally Lipschitz continuous on

\big(\mathbb{R}\times\,]0,T[\,\big)\setminus\big(\,\overline{\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}}\,\cup\,(\{0\}\,\times\,]0,T[\,)\big)
,
and it is continuous on the boundary
\partial\big(\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}\big)\setminus\big(\{0\}\,\times\,]0,T[\big)
;

-

is a classical solution of
u_{t}+f_{l}(u)_{x}=0
on

\big(\,]-\infty,0[\,\times\,]0,T[\,\big)\setminus\,\overline{\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}}
;

-

is a classical solution of
u_{t}+f_{r}(u)_{x}=0
on

\big(\,]0,+\infty[\,\times\,]0,T[\,\big)\setminus\,\overline{\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}}
;

-

is an
AB
-entropy solution of (
1.1
) on
\Delta_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\Gamma_{\mathsf{R}}
;

-

satisfies
the interface entropy condition (
2.13
)
at any point
(0,t)
,
t\in\,]0,T[\,
.

Thus, by Definition
2.2
,
it follows that the function
u(x,t)
in (
5.118
)
provides an

AB
-entropy solution to (
1.1
)
on
\mathbb{R}\times[0,T]
. Moreover,
because of (
5.113
), (
5.118
), (
5.119
),
we have

(5.120)

u(x,0)=u_{0}(x),\qquad\quad u(x,T)=\omega(x)\qquad\ \text{for a.e.}\quad x\in\mathbb{R}\,.

This proves that

(5.121)

\omega=\mathcal{S}_{T}^{[AB]+}u_{0},

and thus

\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{AB}(T)
, which completes
the proof of
the implication
(3)\Rightarrow(1)
of Theorem
4.17
in the case of a non critical connection.

5.5.
Part 2.b -
(3)\Rightarrow(2)
for non critical connections.

As a byproduct of the construction described in §
5.4
,
we show in this Subsection that,
if
\omega
satisfies (
5.89
), (
5.90
),
then
\omega
verifies condition (2) of
Theorem
4.17
,
i.e.
\omega
is a fixed point
of the map
\omega\mapsto\mathcal{{\mathcal{S}}_{\it T}^{\,[{\it AB}\,]+}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{T}^{\,[AB]-}\omega
.
We shall assume that
\omega
satisfies the pointwise constraints of
Case 1
discussed in Remark
4.5
, the other cases being simmetric, or entirely similar,
or simpler.

In order to verify that
\mathcal{{\mathcal{S}}_{\it T}^{\,[{\it AB}\,]+}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{T}^{\,[AB]-}\omega=\omega
,
because of (
5.121
) it is sufficient to prove that,
letting
u_{0}
be the function defined
by (
5.115
), (
5.117
), (
5.119
),
it holds true

(5.122)

u_{0}=\mathcal{S}_{T}^{\,[AB]-}\omega\,.

In turn, recalling the definition (
2.28
) of
AB
backward solution operator, the equality (
5.122
)
is equivalent to the equality

(5.123)

u_{0}(-x)=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{T}^{\,[\overline{B}\,\overline{A}\,]+}\big(\omega(-\ \cdot\,)\big)(x)\qquad x\in\mathbb{R},

where

(5.124)

(x,t)\mapsto\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{t}^{\,[\overline{B}\,\overline{A}\,]+}\big(\omega(-\ \cdot\,)\big)(x)

denotes
the unique
\overline{B}\,\overline{A}
-entropy solution
of

(5.125)

\begin{cases}v_{t}+\overline{f}(x,v)_{x}=0&x\in\mathbb{R},\quad t\geq 0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr v(x,0)=\omega(-x)&x\in\mathbb{R},\end{cases}

\overline{f}(x,v)
being the symmetric flux in (
2.26
).

Towards a proof of (
5.123
), we will determine the solution of (
5.125
) on
\mathbb{R}\times[0,T]
relying on the construction in §
5.4
and on the properties of the
left forward rarefaction-shock wave pattern
derived in §
3.5
.
Observe that the function
u(x,t)
defined by (
5.118
)
for
Case 1
,
with
u_{\mathsf{L}}
,
u_{\mathsf{R}}
defined by (
5.110
), (
5.112
), respectively, is:

-

locally Lipschitz continuous in the region

\mathscr{L}\doteq\big(\,\mathbb{R}\times\,]0,T[\,\big)\setminus\big((\{0\}\,\times\,]0,T[\,)\cup\Gamma[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\big)

where
\Gamma[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
, is defined as in §
3.4
, with
f=f_{r}

(the region
\mathscr{L}
is the complement of the pink region and of the axis
\{x=0\}
in Figure
6
);

-

a classical solution of
u_{t}+f_{l}(u)_{x}=0
on

\,]-\infty,0[\,\times\,]0,T[\,
;

-

a classical solution of
u_{t}+f_{r}(u)_{x}=0
on

\big(\,]0,+\infty[\,\times\,]0,T[\,\big)\setminus\overline{\Gamma[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]}
;

-

satisfies
the interface entropy condition (
2.13
)
at any point
(0,t)
,
t\in\,]0,T[\,
.

Therefore, if we define the transformation
(x,t)\mapsto\alpha(x,t)\doteq(-x,T-t)
, the function

(5.126)

v(x,t)\doteq u(-x,T-t),\qquad(x,t)\in\alpha(\,\overline{\mathscr{L}})\setminus(\{0\}\,\times\,]0,T[\,),

is:

-

an entropy weak solution of
v_{t}+f_{r}(v)_{x}=0
in the open set
\alpha(\mathscr{L})\cap\{x<0\}
;

-

an entropy weak solution of

v_{t}+f_{l}(v)_{x}=0
in the open set
\alpha(\mathscr{L})\cap\{x>0\}
.

On the other hand,
letting
\Delta[\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}],\overline{B},f_{r}]
denote the region defined in (
3.40
) with
\mathsf{L}=y[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
,
A=\overline{B}
, and
f=f_{r}
.
one can directly verify that

(5.127)

\alpha\big(\Gamma[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\big)=\Delta[\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}],\overline{B},f_{r}]\subset\,]-\inf,0[\times\,]0,T[\,.

Notice that
(\mathbb{R}\times[0,T])\setminus(\{0\}\,\times\,]0,T[\,)
is the disjoint union of

\alpha(\,\overline{\mathscr{L}})\setminus(\{0\}\,\times\,]0,T[\,)
and of
\alpha\big(\Gamma[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\big)
.
Then, letting
\mathsf{v}[\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}],\overline{B},f_{r}](x,t)
denote the function defined in (
3.42
), with
\mathsf{L}=y[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
,
A=\overline{B}
, and
f=f_{r}
,
consider the function
v:\mathbb{R}\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}
defined by setting

(5.128)

v(x,t)\doteq\begin{cases}u(-x,T-t),&\text{if \ \ $(x,t)\in\alpha(\,\overline{\mathscr{L}})\setminus(\{0\}\,\times\,]0,T[\,)$},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\mathsf{v}[\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}],\overline{B},f_{r}](x,t),&\text{if \ \ $(x,t)\in\Delta[\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}],\overline{B},f_{r}]$}\,.\end{cases}

By construction and because of the analysis in
§
3.5
, the function
v(x,t)
:

-

is locally Lipschitz continuous on

\big(\mathbb{R}\times\,]0,T[\,\big)\setminus\big(\,\overline{\Delta[\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}],\overline{B},f_{r}]}\,\cup\,(\{0\}\,\times\,]0,T[\,)\big)
,
and it is continuous on the boundary
\partial\big(\Delta[\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}],\overline{B},f_{r}]\big)\setminus\big(\{0\}\,\times\,]0,T[\big)
;

-

is a classical solution of
v_{t}+f_{r}(v)_{x}=0
on

\big(\,]-\infty,0[\,\times\,]0,T[\,\big)\setminus\,\overline{\Delta[\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}],\overline{B},f_{r}]}
;

-

is an entropy weak solution of
v_{t}+f_{r}(v)_{x}=0
on
\Delta[\boldsymbol{y}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}],\overline{B},f_{r}]
;

-

is a classical solution of
v_{t}+f_{l}(v)_{x}=0
on

\,]0,+\infty[\,\times\,]0,T[\,
;

-

satisfies
the
\overline{B}\overline{A}
interface entropy condition,
namely, setting
v_{l}(t)\doteq v(0-,t)
,
v_{r}(t)\doteq v(0+,t)
, and considering the function

I^{\overline{B}\overline{A}}(v_{l},v_{r})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot}}{{=}}\mathrm{sgn}(v_{r}-\overline{A}\,)\left(f_{l}(v_{r})-f_{l}(\,\overline{A}\,)\right)\\
-\mathrm{sgn}(v_{l}-\overline{B}\,)\left(f_{r}(v_{l})-f_{r}(\,\overline{B}\,)\right),

it holds true

(5.129)

f_{r}(v_{l}(t))=f_{l}(v_{r}(t)),\qquad\quad I^{\overline{B}\overline{A}}\big(v_{l}(t),v_{r}(t)\big)\leq 0,\qquad\text{for a.e. $t\in\,]0,T[$\,.}

Notice also that,
because of (
5.120
), (
5.127
), (
5.128
), it follows

(5.130)

v(x,0)=u(-x,T)=\omega(-x)\qquad\ \text{for a.e.}\quad x\in\mathbb{R}\,.

Therefore, by Definition
2.2
,
we deduce that the function
v(x,t)
in (
5.128
)
provides the
\overline{B}\,\overline{A}
-entropy solution to (
5.125
)
on
\mathbb{R}\times[0,T]
, and hence
we have

(5.131)

v(x,t)=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{t}^{\,[\overline{B}\,\overline{A}\,]+}\big(\omega(-\ \cdot\,)\big)(x)\qquad x\in\mathbb{R},\ t\in[0,T]\,.

Moreover, by (
5.120
), (
5.128
), it holds true

\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{T}^{\,[\overline{B}\,\overline{A}\,]+}\big(\omega(-\ \cdot\,)\big)(x)=u(-x,0)=u_{0}(-x)\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}\,,

which proves (
5.123
), and thus concludes the proof of the implication
(3)\Rightarrow(2)
of Theorem
4.17
in the case of a non critical connection.

5.6.
Part 3.a -
(1)\Leftrightarrow(2)
for critical connections

In this Subsection we
rely on the fact

that
the equivalence of conditions (1), (2) of Theorem
4.17
holds for connections which are non critical

(by the proofs in §
5.2
,
5.3
,
5.4
,

5.5
)
,
and we will show that it remains true also for critical connections.
To fix the ideas, throughout this and the following subsections we shall assume that
the connection
(A,B)
is critical at the left, i.e. that

(5.132)

A=\theta_{l}\,,

the case where one assumes that

B=\theta_{r}
being symmetric.
Notice that the assumption
A=\theta_{l}
does not prevent the connection to be critical also at the right, i.e.
B=\theta_{r}
: it might or might not happen.

Notice that there exists
a
sequence

\{A_{n},B_{n}\}_{n}
of non critical connections that satisfy

(5.133)

\lim_{n}(A_{n},B_{n})=(A,B)\,.

We will show only that

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
, since
the reverse implication is clear
(see §
5.1
).
Then, given

\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{[AB]}(T)

with

(5.134)

\omega=\mathcal{S}_{T}^{[AB]+}u_{0},\qquad u_{0}\in\mathbf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\,,

set

(5.135)

\omega_{n}\doteq\mathcal{S}^{[A_{n}B_{n}]+}_{T}u_{0}\,.

Hence, since
\omega_{n}\in\mathcal{A}^{[A_{n}B_{n}]}(T)
,
by the validity of Theorem
4.17
in the non critical case it holds

(5.136)

\omega_{n}=\mathcal{S}^{[A_{n}B_{n}]+}_{T}\circ\mathcal{S}^{[A_{n}B_{n}]-}_{T}\omega_{n}\qquad\forall\;n\,.

Notice that by definition (
2.28
)
it follows that the

\mathbf{L}^{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{loc}}}
stability property (iv) of Theorem
2.8
holds also for the

AB
-backward solution operator
\mathcal{S}^{[AB]-}_{T}
, so that we have

(5.137)

\mathcal{S}^{[A_{n}B_{n}]-}_{T}\omega_{n}\ \ \rightarrow\ \ \mathcal{S}^{[AB]-}_{T}\omega\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathbf{L}^{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{loc}}}(\mathbb{R})\,.

Hence, we deduce that

\omega\stackrel{{\scriptstyle[\text{Thm \ref{theoremsemigroup}-(iv)]}}}{{=}}\lim_{n}\omega_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle[\text{\eqref{eq:noncrifixedn}]}}}{{=}}\lim_{n}\mathcal{S}^{[A_{n}B_{n}]+}_{T}\circ\mathcal{S}^{[A_{n}B_{n}]-}_{T}\omega_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle[\text{Thm \ref{theoremsemigroup}-(iv) and~\eqref{eq:backelleunostab}]}}}{{=}}\mathcal{S}^{[AB]+}_{T}\circ\mathcal{S}^{[AB]-}_{T}\omega\,,

which proves
(1)\Rightarrow(2)
.
∎

5.7.
Part 3.b -
(1)\Rightarrow(3)
for critical connections

In this Subsection
we rely on the fact
that
the implication
(1)\Rightarrow(3)
of Theorem
4.17
holds for connections which are non critical, and in particular
we know (by §
5.2
,
5.3
,
5.4
)
that
Theorems
4.3
,
4.11
,
4.14
,
are verified
for non critical connections.
We will prove that, for a critical connection

(A,B)
,
any element
\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{AB}(T)

satisfies the conditions of
Theorem
4.9
,
or of
Theorem
4.11
, or of Theorem
4.14
.
We divide the proof
in nine steps.

Step 1.

Let
\{A_{n},B_{n}\}_{n}
be a sequence of non critical connections as in Part 3.a.
Given
\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{AB}(T)

as in (
5.134
), and

\omega_{n}
,
as in (
5.135
),
set

(5.138)

u(x,t)\doteq\mathcal{S}^{[AB]+}_{t}u_{0}(x),\qquad t\geq 0,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}\,,

and consider the sequence
u_{n}
of
A_{n}B_{n}
-entropy weak solutions defined by

(5.139)

u_{n}(x,t)\doteq\mathcal{S}^{[A_{n}B_{n}]+}_{t}u_{0}(x),\qquad t\geq 0,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}\,.

Let
u_{n,l},u_{n,r}
denote, respectively, the left and right traces of
u_{n}
at
x=0
defined as in (
2.7
), and let
u_{l},u_{r}
be the left and right traces of
u
at
x=0

(whose existence is derived in Steps 5, 8)
.
Then, by Theorem
2.8

and Corollary
2.11
,
and because of (
5.133
),
it follows

(5.140)

\displaystyle u_{n}(\cdot,t)\ \ \rightarrow\ \ u(\cdot,t)\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathbf{L}^{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{l}oc}}(\mathbb{R})\qquad\forall~t\in[0,T]\,,

(5.141)

\displaystyle f_{l}(u_{n,l})\ \rightharpoonup\ \ f_{l}(u_{l})\qquad\ \text{in}\quad\mathbf{L}^{1}([0,T])\,\,,

(5.142)

\displaystyle f_{r}(u_{n,r})\ \rightharpoonup\ \ f_{r}(u_{r})\quad\ \ \,\text{in}\quad\mathbf{L}^{1}([0,T])\,\,,

and hence, in particular, we have

(5.143)

\omega_{n}\ \ \rightarrow\ \ \omega\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathbf{L}^{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{loc}}}(\mathbb{R})\,.

In order to prove that
\omega
satisfies condition (3) of Theorem
4.17
,
letting

(5.144)

\mathsf{L}\doteq\mathsf{L}[\omega,f_{l}]\,,\qquad\quad\mathsf{R}\doteq\mathsf{R}[\omega,f_{r}],

be quantities defined as in (
4.2
),
we need to show that:

-

If
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
or viceversa, then
\omega
satisfies the conditions of Theorem
4.11
;

-

If
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}=0
, then
\omega
satisfies the conditions of Theorem
4.14
.

-

If
\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
, then
\omega
satisfies the conditions of Theorem
4.9
.

We shall first address the two cases
\mathsf{L}=0,\mathsf{R}>0
, and
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}=0
(the analysis of the case
\mathsf{L}<\mathsf{R}=0
being entirely similar to the one of
\mathsf{L}=0<\mathsf{R}
).
Next, we shall analyze the third case
\mathsf{L}<0<\mathsf{R}
.

Throughout the subsection we let
\mathsf{L}_{n}
,.
\mathsf{R}_{n}
, denote the objects defined as in (
5.144
) for
\omega_{n}
:

(5.145)

\mathsf{L}_{n}\doteq\mathsf{L}[\omega_{n},f_{l}]\,,\quad\qquad\mathsf{R}_{n}\doteq\mathsf{R}[\omega_{n},f_{r}]\,.

Observe that by Remark
2.10
, and because of (
5.143
), the functions
\omega_{n}

have a uniform bound

(5.146)

\|\omega_{n}\|_{{\bf L}^{\infty}}\leq\overline{C}\qquad\quad\forall~n,

for constant
\overline{C}>0
.
Hence, by definition (
4.2
),
the constant
|\mathsf{L}_{n}|

are bounded by
T\cdot\sup_{|u|\leq\overline{C}}|f^{\prime}_{l}(u)|
, and
the constant
\mathsf{R}_{n}

are bounded by
T\cdot\sup_{|u|\leq\overline{C}}|f^{\prime}_{r}(u)|
.
Thus, up to a subsequence, we can
define the limits

(5.147)

\widehat{\mathsf{L}}\doteq\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathsf{L}_{n}\,,\qquad\quad\widehat{\mathsf{R}}\doteq\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathsf{R}_{n}\,.

We claim that

(5.148)

\widehat{\mathsf{L}}\leq{\mathsf{L}},\qquad\quad\widehat{\mathsf{R}}\geq\mathsf{R}\,.

By definition (
4.2
), (
5.144
) of
\mathsf{R}
,
in order to prove the second inequality in (
5.148
),
it is sufficient to show that

(5.149)

\widehat{\mathsf{R}}-T\cdot f^{\prime}_{r}(\omega(\widehat{\mathsf{R}}+))\geq 0\,.

Observe that by Definition
2.2

u_{n}
and
u
are entropy weak solutions of

(5.150)

u_{t}+f_{r}(u)_{x}=0\quad\ x>0,\quad t\in[0,T],

that, because of (
5.140
),
(
5.142
), satisfy the
assumptions (
C.2
), (
C.3
)
of Lemma
C.1
in Appendix
C
.
Thus, applying (
C.4
),
and recalling (
5.134
), (
5.135
), we find

(5.151)

\omega(\widehat{\mathsf{R}}+)\leq\liminf_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\to\infty\\
y\to\widehat{\mathsf{R}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y>0}\end{subarray}}\;\omega_{n}(y+)\,.

Since (
5.147
) and the liminf property imply

(5.152)

\liminf_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\to\infty\\
y\to\widehat{\mathsf{R}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y>0}\end{subarray}}\;\omega_{n}(y+)\leq\liminf_{n}\omega_{n}(\mathsf{R}_{n}+)\,,

we derive from (
5.151
) that

(5.153)

\omega(\widehat{\mathsf{R}}+)\leq\liminf_{n}\omega_{n}(\mathsf{R}_{n}+)\,.

On the other hand, by definition (
4.2
),
(
5.145
)
of
\mathsf{R}_{n}
, it holds

(5.154)

\omega_{n}(\mathsf{R}_{n}+)\leq(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\left(\frac{\mathsf{R}_{n}}{T}\right)\,.

Hence, from (
5.153
), (
5.154
) and (
5.147
)
we deduce

(5.155)

\omega(\widehat{\mathsf{R}}+)\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\left(\frac{\mathsf{R}_{n}}{T}\right)=(f_{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathsf{R}}}{T}\right)\,,

which yields (
5.149
).
This completes the proof of the second inequality in (
5.148
), while the proof of
the first one is entirely similar.

Relying on (
5.148
), we will show in Steps 2-7

the existence of
\omega(0\pm)
, and

that

\omega
satisfies
the
conditions (i)’, (ii)’ of Theorem
4.11
in the case

\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
.
Namely,
in Step 2 we prove (
4.54
),
in Step 3 we prove (
4.56
),
in Step 4 we prove (
4.57
),
in Step 5 we prove (
4.53
)

and the existence of
\omega(0\pm)
,
while in Step 6 we prove (
4.55
).
Finally, in Step 7 we
prove (
4.52
),
concluding the proof of
conditions (i)’, (ii)’ of Theorem
4.11
.
The proof
of the existence of
\omega(0\pm)
, and
that

\omega
satisfies
conditions (i), (ii) of Theorem
4.11
in the case

\mathsf{L}<0
,
\mathsf{R}=0
is entirely similar

to the case
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
, although the symmetry is broken
(because of assumption (
5.132
)),
and it is briefly discussed in Step 8.

Next, in Step 9 we will show
that
\omega
satisfies
conditions (i), (ii) of Theorem
4.14

in
the case
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}=0
.

Finally, in Steps 10-13 we will show
that
\omega
satisfies
conditions (i), (ii) of Theorem
4.9
.

Step 2.

(
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
, proof of (
4.54
):
\omega(x)\geq B
in
]0,\mathsf{R}[\,
)
.

Applying (
4.14
), (
4.15
) of
Theorem
4.3
-(ii) or (
4.54
) of
Theorem
4.11
-(ii)’ for
\omega_{n}
,
in the case of the non critical connections
(A_{n},B_{n})
, we deduce
that

(5.156)

\omega_{n}(x)\geq B_{n}\qquad\quad\forall~x\in\,]0,\mathsf{R}^{n}[\,,\quad\forall~n\,.

On the other hand, by virtue of (
5.143
), (
5.148
),
we can extract a subsequence of
\{\omega_{n}\}
that converges to
\omega
for almost every
x\in\,]0,\mathsf{R}[
. Then, taking the limit in (
5.156
), relying on (
5.133
),

and because of the normalization of

\omega
as a right continuous function (see Remark
4.2
),
we derive (
4.54
).

Step 3.

(
\mathsf{L}=0
,
\mathsf{R}>0
,
proof of (
4.56
):
\mathsf{R}\in\,]0,T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[\ \ \Rightarrow\ \omega(\mathsf{R}+)\leq\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\leq\omega(\mathsf{R}-)
)
.

Observe first that, in the case

\widehat{\mathsf{R}}=\mathsf{R}
,
by the continuity of the function

\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]
with respect to
\mathsf{R}
,
B
(see §
3.1
), and because of (
5.133
), we find

(5.157)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R}_{n},B_{n},f_{r}]=\boldsymbol{u}[\mathsf{R},B,f_{r}]\,.

On the other hand,
if
\mathsf{R}=\widehat{\mathsf{R}}\in\,]0,T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B)[\,
,
we may assume that
\mathsf{R}_{n}\in\,]0,T\cdot f_{r}^{\prime}(B_{n})[\,
,
for
n
sufficiently large.
Hence,
applying
either (
4.13
) of
Theorem
4.3
-(ii), or (
4.56
)
of Theorem
4.11
-(ii)’ for the corresponding
\omega_{n}

in the case of the non critical connections
(A_{n},B_{n})
, we deduce

(5.158)
